A matrix has column space in span ⁡ { 𝒗 t } \spanop\{\bm{v}_{t}\} and row space in
span ⁡ { 𝒌 t } \spanop\{\bm{k}_{t}\} if and only if it is of the form
𝑬 = γ ​ 𝒗 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ \bm{E}=\gamma\,\bm{v}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top} for some γ ∈ ℝ \gamma\in\mathbb{R} .
Admissibility forces
γ ​ ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 = − r t \gamma\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}=-r_{t} , hence
γ = − r t / ( ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 ) \gamma=-r_{t}/(\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}) and
𝑬 = 𝑬 ⋆ \bm{E}=\bm{E}^{\star} .
∎
Every other admissible correction like every DeltaNet-style
correction necessarily writes into at least one key direction
other than 𝒌 t \bm{k}_{t} or one value direction other than 𝒗 t \bm{v}_{t} , i.e. into directions the task never asked about.
A3.4.4 Minimality of interference
Smallness in norm translates into
smallness of the damage done to other associations.
Theorem A4 (Interference caused by the correction) .
Let ( 𝐤 ′ , 𝐯 ′ ) (\bm{k}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}) be any probe pair and let
Δ ⁡ ( 𝐤 ′ , 𝐯 ′ ) := 𝐯 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝐄 ​ 𝐤 ′ \Delta(\bm{k}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}):=\bm{v}^{\prime\top}\bm{E}\bm{k}^{\prime} be the change its read-out
suffers.
(i) Exact form and protection. For 𝑬 = 𝑬 ⋆ \bm{E}=\bm{E}^{\star} ,
Δ ⁡ ( 𝒌 ′ , 𝒗 ′ ) = − r t ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 ​ ( 𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝒗 t ) ​ ( 𝒌 t ⊤ ​ 𝒌 ′ ) , \Delta(\bm{k}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\;=\;-\,\frac{r_{t}}{\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}}\,\big(\bm{v}^{\prime\top}\bm{v}_{t}\big)\big(\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\bm{k}^{\prime}\big)\,,
(A37)
which vanishes whenever 𝒗 ′ ⟂ 𝒗 t \bm{v}^{\prime}\perp\bm{v}_{t} or
𝒌 ′ ⟂ 𝒌 t \bm{k}^{\prime}\perp\bm{k}_{t} , and obeys
| Δ ⁡ ( 𝒌 ′ , 𝒗 ′ ) | ≤ | r t | ​ ‖ 𝒌 ′ ‖ ​ ‖ 𝒗 ′ ‖ / ( ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ ) {{\left|\Delta(\bm{k}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\right|}}\leq{{\left|r_{t}\right|}}\,\left\lVert\bm{k}^{\prime}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{v}^{\prime}\right\rVert/(\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert) .
The condition is a property of the inputs alone: it can be checked in
advance and does not depend on what 𝑺 t \bm{S}_{t} currently holds.
(ii) Minimum expected interference. Suppose probe pairs are
drawn with 𝒌 ′ \bm{k}^{\prime} and 𝒗 ′ \bm{v}^{\prime} independent and isotropic,
E ⁡ [ 𝒌 ′ ​ 𝒌 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ] = σ k 2 ​ 𝑰 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{k}^{\prime}\bm{k}^{\prime\top}]=\sigma_{k}^{2}\bm{I} and
E ⁡ [ 𝒗 ′ ​ 𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ] = σ v 2 ​ 𝑰 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{v}^{\prime}\bm{v}^{\prime\top}]=\sigma_{v}^{2}\bm{I} . Then for every
𝑬 ∈ ℝ d × d \bm{E}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
E ⁡ [ ( 𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝑬 ​ 𝒌 ′ ) 2 ] = σ k 2 ​ σ v 2 ​ ‖ 𝑬 ‖ F 2 . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\big(\bm{v}^{\prime\top}\bm{E}\bm{k}^{\prime}\big)^{2}\Big]\;=\;\sigma_{k}^{2}\,\sigma_{v}^{2}\;\left\lVert\bm{E}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\,.
(A38)
Consequently
𝑬 ⋆ = argmin 𝑬 ∈ ℰ t E ⁡ [ ( 𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝑬 ​ 𝒌 ′ ) 2 ] , min 𝑬 ∈ ℰ t ⁡ E ⁡ [ Δ 2 ] = σ k 2 ​ σ v 2 ​ r t 2 ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 , \bm{E}^{\star}\;=\;\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}_{\bm{E}\in\mathcal{E}_{t}}\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\big(\bm{v}^{\prime\top}\bm{E}\bm{k}^{\prime}\big)^{2}\Big]\,,\qquad\min_{\bm{E}\in\mathcal{E}_{t}}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\Delta^{2}\big]\;=\;\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}\sigma_{v}^{2}\,r_{t}^{2}}{\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}}\,,
(A39)
and the minimizer is unique.
Proof.
(i) Substitute ( A29 ) and use
𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ ( 𝒗 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ ) ​ 𝒌 ′ = ( 𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝒗 t ) ​ ( 𝒌 t ⊤ ​ 𝒌 ′ ) \bm{v}^{\prime\top}(\bm{v}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top})\bm{k}^{\prime}=(\bm{v}^{\prime\top}\bm{v}_{t})(\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\bm{k}^{\prime}) ;
the inequality is Cauchy–Schwarz on each factor.
(ii) Vectorize: with 𝒆 = vec ⁡ ( 𝑬 ) \bm{e}=\vecop(\bm{E}) and 𝒂 ′ = 𝒌 ′ ⊗ 𝒗 ′ \bm{a}^{\prime}=\bm{k}^{\prime}\otimes\bm{v}^{\prime}
we have 𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝑬 ​ 𝒌 ′ = 𝒂 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝒆 \bm{v}^{\prime\top}\bm{E}\bm{k}^{\prime}=\bm{a}^{\prime\top}\bm{e} , hence
E ⁡ [ ( 𝒂 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝒆 ) 2 ] = 𝒆 ⊤ ​ E ​ [ 𝒂 ′ ​ 𝒂 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ] ​ 𝒆 = 𝒆 ⊤ ​ ( E ⁡ [ 𝒌 ′ ​ 𝒌 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ] ⊗ E ⁡ [ 𝒗 ′ ​ 𝒗 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ] ) ​ 𝒆 = σ k 2 ​ σ v 2 ​ ‖ 𝒆 ‖ 2 , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[(\bm{a}^{\prime\top}\bm{e})^{2}\big]\;=\;\bm{e}^{\top}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{a}^{\prime}\bm{a}^{\prime\top}\big]\,\bm{e}\;=\;\bm{e}^{\top}\Big(\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{k}^{\prime}\bm{k}^{\prime\top}]\otimes\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{v}^{\prime}\bm{v}^{\prime\top}]\Big)\bm{e}\;=\;\sigma_{k}^{2}\sigma_{v}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{e}\right\rVert^{2},
(A40)
using independence and ‖ 𝒆 ‖ = ‖ 𝑬 ‖ F \left\lVert\bm{e}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{E}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} . The objective
is therefore a positive multiple of ‖ 𝑬 ‖ F 2 \left\lVert\bm{E}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2} , so
minimizing it over ℰ t \mathcal{E}_{t} is the problem already solved by
Theorem A1 , with the same unique solution.
∎
Part (ii) is what justifies the name: for isotropic probe statistics,
minimum norm and minimum expected interference are literally the
same objective , so the Newton step is minimally invasive in the
operational sense and not merely in the geometric one.
It causes the least expected damage to the rest of the memory.
A3.4.5 Relaxed steps: the correction never moves away from a
correct memory
In practice, β \beta is a learned gate rather than β ⋆ \beta^{\star} . Write
β = λ ​ β ⋆ \beta=\lambda\beta^{\star} , so that the actual update is
𝑺 t + 1 = 𝑺 t + λ ​ 𝑬 ⋆ \bm{S}_{t+1}=\bm{S}_{t}+\lambda\bm{E}^{\star} with relaxation
ω = β ​ ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 \omega=\beta\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2} .
Theorem A5 (Exact Fejér identity) .
Let 𝐒 ∘ \bm{S}^{\circ} be any memory that already satisfies the
addressed association, 𝐯 t ⊤ ​ 𝐒 ∘ ​ 𝐤 t = c t \bm{v}_{t}^{\top}\bm{S}^{\circ}\bm{k}_{t}=c_{t} . Then
‖ 𝑺 t + 1 − 𝑺 ∘ ‖ F 2 = ‖ 𝑺 t − 𝑺 ∘ ‖ F 2 − λ ⁡ ( 2 − λ ) ​ r t 2 ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 . \left\lVert\bm{S}_{t+1}-\bm{S}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;=\;\left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}-\bm{S}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;-\;\lambda\,(2-\lambda)\;\frac{r_{t}^{2}}{\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}}\,.
(A41)
Hence for every λ ∈ ( 0 , 2 ) \lambda\in(0,2) the update strictly decreases
the distance to every correct memory, the decrease is largest at the
Newton step λ = 1 \lambda=1 , and at λ ∈ { 0 , 2 } \lambda\in\{0,2\} the distance is
unchanged.
Proof.
Expand, using ⟨ 𝑬 ⋆ , 𝑺 t − 𝑺 ∘ ⟩ F = − r t ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 ( 𝒗 t ⊤ 𝑺 t 𝒌 t − 𝒗 t ⊤ 𝑺 ∘ 𝒌 t ) = − r t 2 / ( ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 ) = − ‖ 𝑬 ⋆ ‖ F 2 {{\left\langle\bm{E}^{\star},\bm{S}_{t}-\bm{S}^{\circ}\right\rangle}}_{\mathrm{F}}=-\,\tfrac{r_{t}}{\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}}\,\big(\bm{v}_{t}^{\top}\bm{S}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}-\bm{v}_{t}^{\top}\bm{S}^{\circ}\bm{k}_{t}\big)=-\,r_{t}^{2}/(\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2})=-\left\lVert\bm{E}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2} :
‖ 𝑺 t − 𝑺 ∘ + λ ​ 𝑬 ⋆ ‖ F 2 = ‖ 𝑺 t − 𝑺 ∘ ‖ F 2 + 2 ​ λ ​ ⟨ 𝑬 ⋆ , 𝑺 t − 𝑺 ∘ ⟩ F + λ 2 ​ ‖ 𝑬 ⋆ ‖ F 2 , \left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}-\bm{S}^{\circ}+\lambda\bm{E}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}-\bm{S}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\lambda{{\left\langle\bm{E}^{\star},\bm{S}_{t}-\bm{S}^{\circ}\right\rangle}}_{\mathrm{F}}+\lambda^{2}\left\lVert\bm{E}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},
(A42)
which is ( A41 ) after inserting
‖ 𝑬 ⋆ ‖ F 2 = r t 2 / ( ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 ) \left\lVert\bm{E}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=r_{t}^{2}/(\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}) .
∎
This is the strongest sense in which a single write is harmless: it
does not merely perturb other associations little, it moves the memory
closer to every memory that is consistent with the association
being written and that simultaneously, for all of them.
A3.4.6 Greedy minimality is global minimality
Per-step optimality does not by itself imply that a sequence of
such steps is optimal. We show that our does.
Proposition 1 (The limit is the minimum-norm memory) .
Let { ( 𝐤 i , 𝐯 i , c i ) } i = 1 n \{(\bm{k}_{i},\bm{v}_{i},c_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n} be a consistent system of
associations. Apply the rule from 𝐒 0 = 𝟎 \bm{S}_{0}=\bm{0} with relaxation
ω ∈ ( 0 , 2 ) \omega\in(0,2) , visiting each pair infinitely often either cyclically or
by random sampling. Then 𝐒 t \bm{S}_{t} converges to
𝑺 ♯ = argmin { ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F : 𝒗 i ⊤ ​ 𝑺 ​ 𝒌 i = c i ​ for all ​ i } , \bm{S}^{\sharp}\;=\;\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\;:\;\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{k}_{i}=c_{i}\;\;\text{for all }i\Big\}\,,
(A43)
the minimum Frobenius-norm memory satisfying all the
associations.
Proof.
This is the classical convergence theorem for relaxed Kaczmarz
iterations ( Kaczmarz, 1937 ; Cegielski, 2012 ) , applied to the vectorized
system 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒔 = c i \bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}=c_{i} with 𝒂 i = 𝒌 i ⊗ 𝒗 i \bm{a}_{i}=\bm{k}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i} . Each
iterate stays in 𝒔 0 + span ⁡ { 𝒂 1 , … , 𝒂 n } \bm{s}_{0}+\spanop\{\bm{a}_{1},\dots,\bm{a}_{n}\} , which for
𝒔 0 = 𝟎 \bm{s}_{0}=\bm{0} is the row space of the system; the iterates converge
to a solution, and the unique solution in the row space is the
minimum-norm one.
∎
So the memory built by a stream of minimally invasive revisions is
itself the least-norm memory compatible with everything it was asked
to store: locally and globally, the rule writes as little as the task
permits.
A3.4.7 Less invasive than DeltaNet
Theorems A1 – A4 hold given the
requirement encoded in ℰ t \mathcal{E}_{t} : make the scalar read-out of the
addressed pair equal to c t c_{t} , and do nothing else. DeltaNet’s step
has to solve the d d constraints 𝑺 ​ 𝒌 t = 𝒗 t \bm{S}\bm{k}_{t}=\bm{v}_{t} , therefore
its correction is necessarily larger.
The claim here is therefore not that DeltaNet is
suboptimal for its own task, but that the two tasks differ: a
recognition memory needs one scalar corrected, and the Kronecker
Delta Rule corrects it with provably minimal disturbance, whereas a
retrieval memory must rewrite an entire value vector and pays for it
in footprint.
A3.5 Convergence of the KronLSTM memory
We analyze the expected solution and steady state of the randomized
Kronecker Delta Rule with more than d 2 d^{2} associations.
Running memories i.e. online updates are covered via the steady state
considerations.
A3.5.1 Overview
We analyze the Kronecker Delta Rule
𝑺 t + 1 = 𝑺 t − β ⁡ ( 𝒗 i t ⊤ ​ 𝑺 t ​ 𝒌 i t − c i t ) ​ 𝒗 i t ​ 𝒌 i t ⊤ \bm{S}_{t+1}=\bm{S}_{t}-\beta\,(\bm{v}_{i_{t}}^{\top}\bm{S}_{t}\bm{k}_{i_{t}}-c_{i_{t}})\,\bm{v}_{i_{t}}\bm{k}_{i_{t}}^{\top} with randomly drawn indices
when the number of associations exceeds the capacity: n > d 2 n>d^{2} .
The system is then generically inconsistent , and the iterates
no longer converge to a point. Instead we have:
(i) The expected iterate converges linearly to the
sampling-weighted least-squares solution
𝑺 LS = argmin 𝑺 ∑ i p i ​ ( 𝒗 i ⊤ ​ 𝑺 ​ 𝒌 i − c i ) 2 \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}_{\bm{S}}\sum_{i}p_{i}(\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{k}_{i}-c_{i})^{2} . This is for uniform sampling exactly the minimizer of the loss
L \mathrm{L} .
(ii) The iterates converge in distribution to a unique stationary distribution whose mean is exactly
𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} (no bias at any order in β \beta ) and whose
covariance is O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) , given by a Lyapunov-type equation driven
by the residuals at 𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} .
(iii) The steady-state loss exceeds the minimum by the classical LMS
misadjustment,
E ⁡ [ L ] − L min = β 2 ​ E ​ [ ρ i 2 ​ ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ 2 ] + O ⁡ ( β 2 ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\mathrm{L}]-\mathrm{L}_{\min}=\tfrac{\beta}{2}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho_{i}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big]+O(\beta^{2}) ,
where ρ i \rho_{i} are the optimal residuals.
This appendix contains complete proofs of both theorems:
the mean dynamics are solved exactly; existence, uniqueness, and
geometric convergence of the stationary distribution are established
by a synchronous-coupling contraction in the Wasserstein-2 metric;
the stationary mean and the exact covariance equation are derived
with all cross terms accounted for; the misadjustment follows from
an exact trace identity with two-sided bounds; and the transient is
controlled by an explicit geometric-plus-horizon estimate.
Exact convergence can be recovered by diminishing step sizes or by
iterate averaging, and it survives n > d 2 n>d^{2} unharmed whenever the
targets remain consistent. Notably, exact convergence
is always obtain for the correlation target
c i = 𝒗 i ⊤ ​ 𝒌 i c_{i}=\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{k}_{i} and the norm target
c i = ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ 2 c_{i}=\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2} whenever an exact retrieval memory exists.
A3.5.2 Setting and notation
Let n > d 2 n>d^{2} associations ( 𝒌 i , 𝒗 i , c i ) (\bm{k}_{i},\bm{v}_{i},c_{i}) , 1 ≤ i ≤ n 1\leq i\leq n , be
given. Vectorized, with 𝒔 = vec ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) ∈ ℝ d 2 \bm{s}=\vecop(\bm{S})\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} and
𝒂 i = 𝒌 i ⊗ 𝒗 i \bm{a}_{i}=\bm{k}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i} , therefore
‖ 𝒂 i ‖ = ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ \left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert . The associations form
the linear system 𝑨 ​ 𝒔 = 𝒄 \bm{A}\bm{s}=\bm{c} with 𝑨 ∈ ℝ n × d 2 \bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d^{2}}
stacking the rows 𝒂 i ⊤ \bm{a}_{i}^{\top} . We assume 𝑨 \bm{A} has full column rank
d 2 d^{2} i.e. the features span ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} . Since n > d 2 n>d^{2} , the system is
generically inconsistent : no 𝑺 \bm{S} satisfies all associations,
and
L ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) = 1 n ​ ∑ i = 1 n ( 𝒗 i ⊤ ​ 𝑺 ​ 𝒌 i − c i ) 2 \mathrm{L}(\bm{S})\;=\;\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{k}_{i}-c_{i}\big)^{2}
(A44)
has a positive minimum L min > 0 \mathrm{L}_{\min}>0 . At each step an index i t i_{t}
is drawn with probabilities p i > 0 p_{i}>0 , independently of the past, and
the rule is applied with fixed step size β \beta satisfying
0 < β ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 < 2 for all ​ i . 0\;<\;\beta\,\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\;<\;2\qquad\text{for all }i\,.
(A45)
We define
𝑨 ¯ \displaystyle\bar{\bm{A}}\;
: = E ⁡ [ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ≻ 0 , \displaystyle:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\;\succ\;\bm{0}\,,
𝒃 ¯ \displaystyle\bar{\bm{b}}\;
: = E ⁡ [ c ​ 𝒂 ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ c i ​ 𝒂 i , \displaystyle:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c\,\bm{a}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,c_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\,,
(A46)
𝒔 LS \displaystyle\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\;
: = 𝑨 ¯ − 1 ​ 𝒃 ¯ = argmin 𝒔 ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒔 − c i ) 2 , \displaystyle:=\;\bar{\bm{A}}^{-1}\bar{\bm{b}}\;=\;\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}_{\bm{s}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\big(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}-c_{i}\big)^{2}\,,
ρ i \displaystyle\rho_{i}\;
: = c i − 𝒂 i ⊤ 𝒔 LS (optimal residuals) , \displaystyle:=\;c_{i}-\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\quad\text{(optimal residuals)}\,,
(A47)
so that the normal equations read
E [ ρ 𝒂 ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ρ i 𝒂 i = 0 ⟺ 𝒃 ¯ = 𝑨 ¯ 𝒔 LS . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho\,\bm{a}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\rho_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\;=\;\bm{0}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\bar{\bm{b}}\;=\;\bar{\bm{A}}\,\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\,.
(A48)
For uniform sampling, 𝑨 ¯ = 1 n ​ 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 \bar{\bm{A}}=\tfrac{1}{n}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A} and
𝒔 LS = ( 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ) − 1 ​ 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝒄 \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A})^{-1}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{c} is the ordinary
least-squares solution, i.e. the unique minimizer of L \mathrm{L} .
Throughout, expectations without subscript are over i ∼ p i\sim p , and
E π \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi} denotes expectation of a random state distributed
according to a law π \pi . Vectorized, the rule and its error form are
𝒔 t + 1 = 𝑴 i t ​ 𝒔 t + β ​ c i t ​ 𝒂 i t , 𝒆 t + 1 = 𝑴 i t ​ 𝒆 t + β ​ ρ i t ​ 𝒂 i t , 𝑴 i := 𝑰 − β ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ , \bm{s}_{t+1}\;=\;\bm{M}_{i_{t}}\bm{s}_{t}+\beta\,c_{i_{t}}\bm{a}_{i_{t}}\,,\qquad\bm{e}_{t+1}\;=\;\bm{M}_{i_{t}}\,\bm{e}_{t}\;+\;\beta\,\rho_{i_{t}}\,\bm{a}_{i_{t}}\,,\qquad\bm{M}_{i}\;:=\;\bm{I}-\beta\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\,,
(A49)
with 𝒆 t := 𝒔 t − 𝒔 LS \bm{e}_{t}:=\bm{s}_{t}-\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ; the error form follows by
inserting c i = 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒔 LS + ρ i c_{i}=\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}+\rho_{i} . The constants
γ β := min i ⁡ β ⁡ ( 2 − β ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) > 0 , ρ ¯ := γ β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] , σ 2 := E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] \gamma_{\beta}\;:=\;\min_{i}\,\beta\,\big(2-\beta\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big)\;>\;0\,,\qquad\bar{\rho}\;:=\;\gamma_{\beta}\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\;\in\;(0,1]\,,\qquad\sigma^{2}\;:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\big]
(A50)
appear throughout ( 0 < ρ ¯ ≤ 1 0<\bar{\rho}\leq 1 is shown in
Lemma A1 ).
A3.5.3 Theorems on expected and steady state solution
Theorem A6 (Expected solution: exact, unbiased least squares) .
Under ( A45 ), the expected iterate obeys the exact
deterministic recursion
E [ 𝒔 t + 1 ] = ( 𝑰 − β 𝑨 ¯ ) E [ 𝒔 t ] + β 𝒃 ¯ ⟺ E [ 𝒆 t + 1 ] = ( 𝑰 − β 𝑨 ¯ ) E [ 𝒆 t ] , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{s}_{t+1}\big]\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}}\big)\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{s}_{t}\big]\;+\;\beta\,\bar{\bm{b}}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{e}_{t+1}\big]\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}}\big)\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{e}_{t}\big]\,,
(A51)
whose unique fixed point is 𝐬 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} , and
‖ E ⁡ [ 𝑺 t ] − 𝑺 LS ‖ F ≤ r t ​ ‖ 𝑺 0 − 𝑺 LS ‖ F , r := max k ⁡ | 1 − β ​ λ k ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) | < 1 , \left\lVert\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{S}_{t}]-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\;\leq\;r^{t}\,\left\lVert\bm{S}_{0}-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\,,\qquad r\;:=\;\max_{k}\,{{\left|1-\beta\lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{A}})\right|}}\;<\;1\,,
(A52)
so E ⁡ [ 𝐒 t ] → 𝐒 LS \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{S}_{t}]\to\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} linearly. The convergence of the
mean is exactly unbiased: the fixed point of ( A51 )
is 𝐬 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} itself, with no O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) correction.
Theorem A7 (Steady state: stationary distribution around
𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ) .
Under ( A45 ), the iterates form a Markov chain on
ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} that has a unique stationary distribution π β \pi_{\beta}
with finite second moment, and the law of 𝐬 t \bm{s}_{t} converges to
π β \pi_{\beta} geometrically in the Wasserstein-2 metric:
𝒲 2 ​ ( law ​ ( 𝒔 t ) , π β ) ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) t / 2 ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( law ​ ( 𝒔 0 ) , π β ) . \mathcal{W}_{2}\big(\text{law}(\bm{s}_{t}),\,\pi_{\beta}\big)\;\leq\;\big(1-\bar{\rho}\big)^{t/2}\;\mathcal{W}_{2}\big(\text{law}(\bm{s}_{0}),\,\pi_{\beta}\big)\,.
(A53)
Under π β \pi_{\beta} , with 𝐞 := 𝐬 − 𝐬 LS \bm{e}:=\bm{s}-\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} :
(i) Mean: E π β ​ [ 𝑺 ] = 𝑺 LS \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\bm{S}]=\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} exactly.
(ii) Covariance: 𝑽 β := E π β ​ [ 𝒆 ​ 𝒆 ⊤ ] \bm{V}_{\beta}:=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}] satisfies the exact stationarity equation
𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β + 𝑽 β ​ 𝑨 ¯ − β ​ E ​ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] = β ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] , \bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}+\bm{V}_{\beta}\bar{\bm{A}}\;-\;\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\,\bm{a}\big)\,\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\;=\;\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\,\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\,,
(A54)
and obeys the two-sided trace bounds
β ​ σ 2 2 ≤ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) ≤ β ​ σ 2 2 − β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 , \displaystyle\frac{\beta\,\sigma^{2}}{2}\;\leq\;\tr\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}\big)\;\leq\;\frac{\beta\,\sigma^{2}}{2-\beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}}\,,
(A55)
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 − 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 ] ≤ β ​ σ 2 ( 2 − β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ; \displaystyle\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\!\left[\left\lVert\bm{S}-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]\;\leq\;\frac{\beta\,\sigma^{2}}{\big(2-\beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big)\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,;
in particular 𝑽 β = O ⁡ ( β ) \bm{V}_{\beta}=O(\beta) , and to leading order
( A54 ) is the Lyapunov equation
𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 + 𝑽 ​ 𝑨 ¯ = β ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] \bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}+\bm{V}\bar{\bm{A}}=\beta\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}] .
(iii) Steady-state loss (misadjustment): for uniform sampling,
E π β ​ [ L ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) ] − L min = tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) = β 2 ​ ( σ 2 + E ⁡ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] ) , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\mathrm{L}(\bm{S})\big]\;-\;\mathrm{L}_{\min}\;=\;\tr\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}\big)\;=\;\frac{\beta}{2}\Big(\sigma^{2}+\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}\big)\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\big]\Big)\,,
(A56)
an exact identity, whence
β 2 ​ σ 2 ≤ E π β ​ [ L ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) ] − L min ≤ β 2 ​ σ 2 ⋅ 2 2 − β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 , σ 2 = E ⁡ [ ρ i 2 ​ ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ 2 ] , \frac{\beta}{2}\,\sigma^{2}\;\leq\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\mathrm{L}(\bm{S})\big]-\mathrm{L}_{\min}\;\leq\;\frac{\beta}{2}\,\sigma^{2}\cdot\frac{2}{2-\beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}}\,,\qquad\sigma^{2}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho_{i}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big]\,,
(A57)
i.e. E π β ​ [ L ] = L min + β 2 ​ σ 2 + O ⁡ ( β 2 ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\mathrm{L}]=\mathrm{L}_{\min}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}\sigma^{2}+O(\beta^{2}) . This is the classical Widrow–Hoff LMS
misadjustment for the bilinear feature 𝒂 = 𝒌 ⊗ 𝒗 \bm{a}=\bm{k}\otimes\bm{v} .
(iv) Transient: with 𝒘 := E ⁡ [ ρ ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ​ 𝒂 ] \bm{w}:=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\bm{a}\big] ,
E ​ ‖ 𝒆 t ‖ 2 ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ 2 ) t ​ ‖ 𝒆 0 ‖ 2 + 2 ​ β 2 ​ σ 2 ρ ¯ + 4 ​ β 4 ​ ‖ 𝒘 ‖ 2 ρ ¯ 2 , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\;\leq\;\Big(1-\tfrac{\bar{\rho}}{2}\Big)^{t}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{0}\right\rVert^{2}\;+\;\frac{2\beta^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\bar{\rho}}\;+\;\frac{4\beta^{4}\left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert^{2}}{\bar{\rho}^{2}}\,,
(A58)
a geometric approach to an O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) horizon (note ρ ¯ = Θ ⁡ ( β ) \bar{\rho}=\Theta(\beta) ): overload does not slow the transient, it only
replaces the limit point by a limit distribution.
A3.5.4 Proofs of the theorems
Throughout, ℱ t := σ ⁡ ( 𝒔 0 , i 0 , … , i t − 1 ) \mathcal{F}_{t}:=\sigma(\bm{s}_{0},i_{0},\dots,i_{t-1}) denotes the
filtration of the past; i t i_{t} is independent of ℱ t \mathcal{F}_{t} by
assumption. We repeatedly use that ( A49 ) is linear
in the state with coefficients independent of the state.
Two lemmas crucial for the proofs of the theorems.
Lemma A1 (One-step mean-square contraction of the homogeneous
part) .
For every fixed 𝐱 ∈ ℝ d 2 \bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} ,
E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒙 ‖ 2 ] = ‖ 𝒙 ‖ 2 − ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ β ​ ( 2 − β ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒙 ) 2 ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) ​ ‖ 𝒙 ‖ 2 , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\,\bm{x}\right\rVert^{2}\big]\;=\;\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert^{2}\;-\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\beta\big(2-\beta\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big)\big(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{x}\big)^{2}\;\leq\;\big(1-\bar{\rho}\big)\,\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert^{2}\,,
(A59)
and ρ ¯ ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] \bar{\rho}\in(0,1] .
Proof.
Since 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} is symmetric,
‖ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒙 ‖ 2 = 𝒙 ⊤ ​ ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ) 2 ​ 𝒙 = ‖ 𝒙 ‖ 2 − 2 ​ β ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒙 ) 2 + β 2 ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒙 ) 2 , \left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\bm{x}\right\rVert^{2}\;=\;\bm{x}^{\top}\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\big)^{2}\bm{x}\;=\;\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert^{2}-2\beta\big(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{x}\big)^{2}+\beta^{2}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{x}\big)^{2}\,,
(A60)
which is the equality in ( A59 ) after averaging over
i ∼ p i\sim p . By ( A45 ), β ⁡ ( 2 − β ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) ≥ γ β > 0 \beta(2-\beta\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2})\geq\gamma_{\beta}>0 for every i i , and
∑ i p i ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒙 ) 2 = 𝒙 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒙 ≥ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ ‖ 𝒙 ‖ 2 \sum_{i}p_{i}(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{x})^{2}=\bm{x}^{\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{x}\geq\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert^{2} , hence the inequality with
ρ ¯ = γ β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) \bar{\rho}=\gamma_{\beta}\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}) . Finally
ρ ¯ ≤ 1 \bar{\rho}\leq 1 because the left-hand side of ( A59 )
is nonnegative for every 𝒙 \bm{x} . We assumed that
𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} has full rank, therefore
0 < λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) 0<\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}) and with 0 < γ β 0<\gamma_{\beta} we have
0 < ρ ¯ 0<\bar{\rho} .
∎
Lemma A2 (The chain lives in 𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} and its kernel is a
𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} -contraction) .
Let P P denote the transition kernel of ( A49 ) and let
𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} be the space of probability laws on ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} with finite
second moment, equipped with the Wasserstein-2 metric 𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} . Then
P P maps 𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} into 𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} , and for all μ , ν ∈ 𝒫 2 \mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2} ,
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) ≤ 1 − ρ ¯ ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , ν ) . \mathcal{W}_{2}\big(\mu P,\,\nu P\big)\;\leq\;\sqrt{1-\bar{\rho}}\;\,\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu)\,.
(A61)
Consequently P P has a unique invariant law π β ∈ 𝒫 2 \pi_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
and ( A53 ) holds.
Proof.
P P preserves 𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} : for 𝒆 ∼ μ ∈ 𝒫 2 \bm{e}\sim\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2} and an
independent index i i ,
E ​ ‖ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ≤ 2 ​ E ​ ‖ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 ‖ 2 + 2 ​ β 2 ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] ≤ 2 ​ E ​ ‖ 𝒆 ‖ 2 + 2 ​ β 2 ​ σ 2 < ∞ \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\bm{e}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 2\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\bm{e}\right\rVert^{2}+2\beta^{2}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}]\leq 2\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{e}\right\rVert^{2}+2\beta^{2}\sigma^{2}<\infty ,
using Lemma A1 .
Contraction: let ( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y}) be an optimal 𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} -coupling of
( μ , ν ) (\mu,\nu) and couple the two chains synchronously : draw one
index i i (independent of ( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y}) ) and set
𝒙 ′ = 𝑴 i ​ 𝒙 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i \bm{x}^{\prime}=\bm{M}_{i}\bm{x}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i} ,
𝒚 ′ = 𝑴 i ​ 𝒚 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i \bm{y}^{\prime}=\bm{M}_{i}\bm{y}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i} . The additive terms cancel in
the difference,
𝒙 ′ − 𝒚 ′ = 𝑴 i ​ ( 𝒙 − 𝒚 ) , \bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\;=\;\bm{M}_{i}\,(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\,,
(A62)
so by Lemma A1 , first conditionally on ( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y})
and then in expectation,
E ​ ‖ 𝒙 ′ − 𝒚 ′ ‖ 2 ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) ​ E ​ ‖ 𝒙 − 𝒚 ‖ 2 = ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , ν ) 2 . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}\;\leq\;(1-\bar{\rho})\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right\rVert^{2}\;=\;(1-\bar{\rho})\,\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu)^{2}\,.
(A63)
Since ( 𝒙 ′ , 𝒚 ′ ) (\bm{x}^{\prime},\bm{y}^{\prime}) is some coupling of ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) (\mu P,\nu P) ,
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) 2 ≤ E ​ ‖ 𝒙 ′ − 𝒚 ′ ‖ 2 \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu P,\nu P)^{2}\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2} , which is
( A61 ).
Conclusion: ( 𝒫 2 , 𝒲 2 ) (\mathcal{P}_{2},\mathcal{W}_{2}) is a complete metric space (see,
e.g., ( Villani, 2009 , Theorem 6.18) ), so Banach’s fixed-point theorem yields a unique
π β ∈ 𝒫 2 \pi_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{2} with π β ​ P = π β \pi_{\beta}P=\pi_{\beta} and
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ ​ P t , π β ) ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) t / 2 ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , π β ) \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu P^{t},\pi_{\beta})\leq(1-\bar{\rho})^{t/2}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\pi_{\beta}) for every μ ∈ 𝒫 2 \mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2} , which is ( A53 ).
Convergence in 𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} implies convergence in distribution together
with convergence of first and second moments.
∎
Comments to the proof of Lemma A2 in 9 bullet points.
∙ \bullet 1) Overview.
How a mixture of non-contractive matrices produces a
contraction: the kernel P P and the coupling argument.
The matrices 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} are not themselves contractions - by
Lemma A1 , ‖ 𝑴 i ‖ 2 = 1 \left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}=1 for every single
i i , since 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} has eigenvalue 1 1 on 𝒂 i ⟂ \bm{a}_{i}^{\perp} . Contractivity
in Lemma A2 is a property of the mixture P P , not of
any individual 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} , and P P is not a matrix at all: it is a
Markov kernel acting on laws in 𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} , and the relevant notion of
size for it is the Wasserstein metric 𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} on 𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} itself -
not any norm on ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} from which P P ’s contraction could be
read off the 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} ’s directly. This note spells out how P P is
built from the 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} ’s, in what sense P P does and does not have an
“operator norm,” and why the coupling argument nonetheless produces
the contraction ( A61 ).
∙ \bullet 2) Constructing P P from the 𝐌 i \bm{M}_{i} .
For a fixed state 𝒙 ∈ ℝ d 2 \bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} , define the n n deterministic
affine maps
Φ i ( 𝒙 ) := 𝑴 i 𝒙 + β ρ i 𝒂 i , i = 1 , … , n . \Phi_{i}(\bm{x})\;:=\;\bm{M}_{i}\bm{x}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\,,\qquad i=1,\dots,n\,.
(A64)
One step of the chain from 𝒙 \bm{x} lands at Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) \Phi_{i}(\bm{x}) with
probability p i p_{i} , so the transition kernel is the mixture of point
masses
P ⁡ ( 𝒙 , ⋅ ) := ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ δ Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) ​ ( ⋅ ) , P(\bm{x},\cdot)\;:=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\delta_{\Phi_{i}(\bm{x})}(\cdot)\,,
(A65)
and, for a law μ \mu , μ ​ P \mu P is defined by
( μ ​ P ) ​ ( B ) = ∫ P ⁡ ( 𝒙 , B ) ​ μ ​ ( 𝑑 𝒙 ) (\mu P)(B)=\int P(\bm{x},B)\,\mu(d\bm{x}) - equivalently, μ ​ P \mu P
is the law of Φ I ​ ( 𝑿 ) \Phi_{I}(\bm{X}) for 𝑿 ∼ μ \bm{X}\sim\mu and I ∼ p I\sim p drawn
independently of 𝑿 \bm{X} .
B B is a Borel (measurable) subset of ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} - an arbitrary
test set , held fixed, to which the kernel assigns a
probability. Concretely,
P ( 𝒙 , B ) := Pr ( Φ I ( 𝒙 ) ∈ B ) = ∑ i = 1 n p i 1 [ Φ i ( 𝒙 ) ∈ B ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i δ Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) ( B ) , P(\bm{x},B)\;:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{Pr}}\big(\Phi_{I}(\bm{x})\in B\big)\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\;\mathbbm{1}\big[\Phi_{i}(\bm{x})\in B\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\;\delta_{\Phi_{i}(\bm{x})}(B)\,,
(A66)
where Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) = 𝑴 i ​ 𝒙 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i \Phi_{i}(\bm{x})=\bm{M}_{i}\bm{x}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i} is the (fixed,
deterministic) image of 𝒙 \bm{x} under transition i i , and I ∼ p I\sim p is
the random index selecting which transition fires. In words:
P ⁡ ( 𝒙 , B ) P(\bm{x},B) is the probability that one step of the chain, started at
𝒙 \bm{x} , lands in B B - obtained by summing the weights p i p_{i} of
every index i i whose image Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) \Phi_{i}(\bm{x}) happens to fall inside B B .
P P as a function of each argument separately.
The kernel P : ℝ d 2 × ℬ ⁡ ( ℝ d 2 ) → [ 0 , 1 ] P:\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}})\to[0,1] plays a
different role in each of its two slots:
•
Fixed 𝒙 \bm{x} , varying B B : P ⁡ ( 𝒙 , ⋅ ) P(\bm{x},\cdot) is itself a
probability measure on ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} - concretely, the discrete
measure
P ⁡ ( 𝒙 , ⋅ ) = ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ δ Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) ​ ( ⋅ ) P(\bm{x},\cdot)\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\delta_{\Phi_{i}(\bm{x})}(\cdot)
(A67)
putting mass p i p_{i} on the point Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) \Phi_{i}(\bm{x}) for each i i (masses
are summed if several Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) \Phi_{i}(\bm{x}) coincide). This is the law of
the chain’s next state, given that it is currently at 𝒙 \bm{x} .
•
Fixed B B , varying 𝒙 \bm{x} : P ⁡ ( ⋅ , B ) P(\cdot,B) is a bounded
measurable function of 𝒙 \bm{x} , since each indicator
𝟙 [ Φ i ( 𝒙 ) ∈ B ] \mathbbm{1}[\Phi_{i}(\bm{x})\in B] is measurable in 𝒙 \bm{x} and the sum is
finite.
This is the standard convention for a Markov transition kernel:
measurable in the first argument, a probability measure in the
second.
∙ \bullet 3) the pushforward measure μ ​ P \mu P is well defined.
For a law μ \mu of the current state, the one-step-ahead law μ ​ P \mu P
is defined by integrating the second argument B B (held fixed)
against μ \mu acting on the first argument 𝒙 \bm{x} :
( μ ​ P ) ​ ( B ) := ∫ ℝ d 2 P ⁡ ( 𝒙 , B ) ​ μ ​ ( 𝑑 𝒙 ) . (\mu P)(B)\;:=\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}}P(\bm{x},B)\;\mu(d\bm{x})\,.
(A68)
Equivalently, μ ​ P \mu P is the law of Φ I ​ ( 𝑿 ) \Phi_{I}(\bm{X}) for 𝑿 ∼ μ \bm{X}\sim\mu
and I ∼ p I\sim p drawn independently of 𝑿 \bm{X} : draw a random current
state 𝑿 \bm{X} , draw an independent random index I I , and output
Φ I ​ ( 𝑿 ) \Phi_{I}(\bm{X}) . The two roles of B B above are exactly what make this
integral meaningful: for each fixed 𝒙 \bm{x} in the integral,
P ⁡ ( 𝒙 , B ) P(\bm{x},B) is a number in [ 0 , 1 ] [0,1] (the probability mass the
one-step law from 𝒙 \bm{x} places on B B ); integrating that number
against μ ⁡ ( d ​ 𝒙 ) \mu(d\bm{x}) averages it over the randomness in the current
state, and the result, as a function of B B , is again a probability
measure. It is the law μ ​ P \mu P appearing throughout the steady-state
analysis of the rule.
∙ \bullet 4) P P has no operator norm on ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} .
Thus P P is linear as an operator on (signed) measures
( ( α ​ μ + ( 1 − α ) ​ ν ) ​ P = α ​ μ ​ P + ( 1 − α ) ​ ν ​ P (\alpha\mu+(1-\alpha)\nu)P=\alpha\mu P+(1-\alpha)\nu P ),
but this linearity lives on the infinite-dimensional space of
measures, not on ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} : there is no d 2 × d 2 d^{2}\times d^{2} matrix
representing P P , so P P has no operator norm on
( ℝ d 2 , ‖ ⋅ ‖ ) (\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}},\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert) the way each 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} does. What P P does have is an operator norm in the sense appropriate to its
actual domain: a Lipschitz modulus on the metric space
( 𝒫 2 , 𝒲 2 ) (\mathcal{P}_{2},\mathcal{W}_{2}) , namely the smallest L L such that
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) ≤ L ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , ν ) \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu P,\nu P)\leq L\,\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu) for all μ , ν ∈ 𝒫 2 \mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2} . That modulus is exactly what ( A61 ) bounds by
1 − ρ ¯ < 1 \sqrt{1-\bar{\rho}}<1 . It cannot be read off a per-map bound
such as max i ⁡ ‖ 𝑴 i ‖ 2 \max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\right\rVert_{2} , which equals 1 1 by
Lemma A1 and would certify no contraction whatsoever;
obtaining a modulus strictly below 1 1 requires the coupling argument
below, precisely because it is a statement about P P acting on laws,
not about any 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} acting on ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} .
∙ \bullet 5) The Wasserstein metric is essential to prove the contraction.
The definition
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , ν ) 2 = inf γ ∈ Γ ⁡ ( μ , ν ) E γ ​ [ ‖ 𝑿 − 𝒀 ‖ 2 ] \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu)^{2}\;=\;\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma(\mu,\nu)}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\left\lVert\bm{X}-\bm{Y}\right\rVert^{2}\big]
(A69)
is an infimum over all couplings γ \gamma of ( μ , ν ) (\mu,\nu)
(joint laws with the prescribed marginals). This has an immediate but
crucial consequence: to upper-bound 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu P,\nu P) it suffices
to exhibit any single coupling of ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) (\mu P,\nu P) and compute
its second moment. Note that we never need the optimal coupling of the
outputs, let alone an explicit formula for P P as an operator.
∙ \bullet 6) The synchronous coupling.
We are free to choose how the randomness that drives one step of
each chain is generated, and we exploit that freedom by reusing
the same random index for both chains. Concretely: let ( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y})
be an optimal coupling of ( μ , ν ) (\mu,\nu) , i.e. a joint law with
𝒙 ∼ μ \bm{x}\sim\mu , 𝒚 ∼ ν \bm{y}\sim\nu , and
E ​ ‖ 𝒙 − 𝒚 ‖ 2 = 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , ν ) 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right\rVert^{2}=\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu)^{2} (such a coupling exists
because ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} is Polish; see ( Villani, 2009 , Theorem 4.1) ).
Draw a single index
i ∼ p i\sim p , independent of ( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y}) , and set
𝒙 ′ := Φ i ​ ( 𝒙 ) = 𝑴 i ​ 𝒙 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i , 𝒚 ′ := Φ i ​ ( 𝒚 ) = 𝑴 i ​ 𝒚 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i . \bm{x}^{\prime}\;:=\;\Phi_{i}(\bm{x})\;=\;\bm{M}_{i}\bm{x}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\,,\qquad\bm{y}^{\prime}\;:=\;\Phi_{i}(\bm{y})\;=\;\bm{M}_{i}\bm{y}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\,.
(A70)
Marginally, 𝒙 ′ ∼ μ ​ P \bm{x}^{\prime}\sim\mu P and 𝒚 ′ ∼ ν ​ P \bm{y}^{\prime}\sim\nu P by the very
definition ( A65 ) of μ ​ P \mu P , applied separately to each
starting law; hence ( 𝒙 ′ , 𝒚 ′ ) (\bm{x}^{\prime},\bm{y}^{\prime}) is a valid coupling of
( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) (\mu P,\nu P) . This coupling is not necessarily the optimal one,
but that is all the argument requires.
∙ \bullet 7) The additive target terms cancel.
Because 𝒙 ′ \bm{x}^{\prime} and 𝒚 ′ \bm{y}^{\prime} are built from the same draw i i , the
inhomogeneous term β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i \beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i} is identical in both and cancels
exactly in the difference:
𝒙 ′ − 𝒚 ′ = ( 𝑴 i ​ 𝒙 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ) − ( 𝑴 i ​ 𝒚 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ) = 𝑴 i ​ ( 𝒙 − 𝒚 ) . \bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\;=\;\big(\bm{M}_{i}\bm{x}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\big)-\big(\bm{M}_{i}\bm{y}+\beta\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\big)\;=\;\bm{M}_{i}\,(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\,.
(A71)
The part of the update that cancels out is exactly the part
carrying the targets c i c_{i} .
This is the crux of the construction: it converts a statement about
two inhomogeneous, randomly driven affine updates into a
statement about a single homogeneous linear map, 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} ,
applied to the fixed vector 𝒙 − 𝒚 \bm{x}-\bm{y} . This is exactly the object
Lemma A1 controls. Had we instead coupled the two
chains using independent indices for 𝒙 ′ \bm{x}^{\prime} and 𝒚 ′ \bm{y}^{\prime} , the
noise terms would not cancel and the argument would not go through;
the whole content of the synchronous-coupling trick is this
cancellation.
∙ \bullet 8) Deriving the contraction.
Since i i is independent of ( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y}) , condition on ( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y})
first and average over i i :
E [ ‖ 𝒙 ′ − 𝒚 ′ ‖ 2 | 𝒙 , 𝒚 ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ‖ 𝑴 i ( 𝒙 − 𝒚 ) ‖ 2 ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) ‖ 𝒙 − 𝒚 ‖ 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}\,\big|\,\bm{x},\bm{y}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}(\bm{x}-\bm{y})\right\rVert^{2}\;\leq\;\big(1-\bar{\rho}\big)\,\left\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right\rVert^{2}
(A72)
by Lemma A1 , applied pointwise to the vector 𝒙 − 𝒚 \bm{x}-\bm{y} (fixed once we condition). Taking the outer expectation over
( 𝒙 , 𝒚 ) (\bm{x},\bm{y}) and using that this coupling was chosen optimal,
E ​ ‖ 𝒙 ′ − 𝒚 ′ ‖ 2 ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) ​ E ​ ‖ 𝒙 − 𝒚 ‖ 2 = ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , ν ) 2 . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}\;\leq\;\big(1-\bar{\rho}\big)\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right\rVert^{2}\;=\;\big(1-\bar{\rho}\big)\,\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu)^{2}\,.
(A73)
Finally, since ( 𝒙 ′ , 𝒚 ′ ) (\bm{x}^{\prime},\bm{y}^{\prime}) is a coupling of ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) (\mu P,\nu P) , the infimum defining 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) 2 \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu P,\nu P)^{2} is at most the cost
of this particular coupling:
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ ​ P , ν ​ P ) 2 ≤ E ​ ‖ 𝒙 ′ − 𝒚 ′ ‖ 2 ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ ) ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ , ν ) 2 , \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu P,\nu P)^{2}\;\leq\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}\;\leq\;\big(1-\bar{\rho}\big)\,\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu)^{2}\,,
(A74)
and taking square roots recovers ( A61 ) exactly.
∙ \bullet 9) Summary of the approach.
Three ingredients combine, none of which is a bound on P P as an
operator on ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} : (1) 𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} is an infimum over couplings, so
any one coupling of the images gives a valid upper bound; (2) the
synchronous coupling is reusing the same random index for both
copies and is a legitimate choice precisely because P P is defined by
mixing over that same randomness, and it makes the target-dependent
noise cancel identically; (3) what remains is a bound on
E i ​ ‖ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒛 ‖ 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\bm{z}\right\rVert^{2} for the fixed vector 𝒛 = 𝒙 − 𝒚 \bm{z}=\bm{x}-\bm{y} , which is exactly the pointwise mean-square contraction already
established in Lemma A1 . This lemma is a statement purely about
the 𝑴 i \bm{M}_{i} ’s and 𝑨 ¯ = ∑ i p i ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ \bar{\bm{A}}=\sum_{i}p_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top} , requiring
no coupling of its own. The contraction of P P in 𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} is thus
assembled entirely from the per-index contraction of
Lemma A1 , transported to the space of distributions
by the coupling device. It is not, and could not be, a statement
recoverable from max i ⁡ ‖ 𝑴 i ‖ 2 \max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\right\rVert_{2} alone.
Proof of Theorem A6 .
Proof.
Exact mean recursion. The update ( A49 ) is linear in
𝒔 t \bm{s}_{t} , and i t i_{t} is independent of ℱ t ⊇ σ ⁡ ( 𝒔 t ) \mathcal{F}_{t}\supseteq\sigma(\bm{s}_{t}) . Hence, conditioning on ℱ t \mathcal{F}_{t} ,
E ⁡ [ 𝒔 t + 1 | ℱ t ] = E ⁡ [ 𝑴 i t ] ​ 𝒔 t + β ​ E ​ [ c i t ​ 𝒂 i t ] = ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ 𝒔 t + β ​ 𝒃 ¯ , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{s}_{t+1}\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{M}_{i_{t}}\big]\,\bm{s}_{t}+\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c_{i_{t}}\bm{a}_{i_{t}}\big]\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}}\big)\,\bm{s}_{t}+\beta\,\bar{\bm{b}}\,,
(A75)
and the tower property gives ( A51 ).
This equation holds with equality and is
not an approximation: no linearization was needed because the update
itself is linear. Subtracting the fixed-point relation
𝒔 LS = ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ 𝒔 LS + β ​ 𝒃 ¯ \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}})\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}+\beta\bar{\bm{b}} , which holds by the normal equations ( A48 )
( 𝒃 ¯ = 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS \bar{\bm{b}}=\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ), yields the error form and,
by induction,
E ⁡ [ 𝒆 t ] = ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ) t ​ E ​ [ 𝒆 0 ] . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{e}_{t}\big]\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}}\big)^{t}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{e}_{0}\big]\,.
(A76)
Spectral radius. 𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} is symmetric positive definite;
let 0 < λ min = λ 1 ≤ ⋯ ≤ λ d 2 = λ max 0<\lambda_{\min}=\lambda_{1}\leq\dots\leq\lambda_{d^{2}}=\lambda_{\max} be its eigenvalues. For any unit vector 𝒙 \bm{x} ,
𝒙 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒙 = ∑ i p i ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒙 ) 2 ≤ max i ⁡ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 \bm{x}^{\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{x}=\sum_{i}p_{i}(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{x})^{2}\leq\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2} , so
β ​ λ max ≤ β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 < 2 \beta\,\lambda_{\max}\;\leq\;\beta\,\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\;<\;2
(A77)
by ( A45 ). Hence, every eigenvalue 1 − β ​ λ k 1-\beta\lambda_{k}
of 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ \bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}} lies in ( − 1 , 1 ) (-1,1) :
1 − β ​ λ k < 1 1-\beta\lambda_{k}<1 since β ​ λ k > 0 \beta\lambda_{k}>0 , and
1 − β ​ λ k > − 1 1-\beta\lambda_{k}>-1 since β ​ λ k < 2 \beta\lambda_{k}<2 . The matrix is
symmetric, so its spectral norm equals
r = max k ⁡ | 1 − β ​ λ k | < 1 r=\max_{k}|1-\beta\lambda_{k}|<1 and
‖ E ⁡ [ 𝒆 t ] ‖ ≤ r t ​ ‖ 𝒆 0 ‖ \left\lVert\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}_{t}]\right\rVert\leq r^{t}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{0}\right\rVert , i.e. linear convergence of
E ⁡ [ 𝑺 t ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{S}_{t}] to 𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} in Frobenius norm ( ‖ 𝒔 ‖ 2 = ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F \left\lVert\bm{s}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} ).
Exact unbiasedness. The fixed point of the affine map 𝒛 ↦ ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ 𝒛 + β ​ 𝒃 ¯ \bm{z}\mapsto(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}})\bm{z}+\beta\bar{\bm{b}} is unique
( β ​ 𝑨 ¯ \beta\bar{\bm{A}} invertible) and equals 𝑨 ¯ − 1 ​ 𝒃 ¯ = 𝒔 LS \bar{\bm{A}}^{-1}\bar{\bm{b}}=\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} for every β \beta in the admissible range: the
limit of the mean does not depend on β \beta and carries no
O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) correction. ∎
Proof of Theorem A7 .
Proof.
Existence, uniqueness, membership in 𝒫 2 \mathcal{P}_{2} , and the geometric
𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} -convergence ( A53 ) are Lemma A2 .
We have to prove (i)–(iv). In stationarity we write 𝒆 ∼ π β \bm{e}\sim\pi_{\beta} (centered at 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ), draw a fresh index i i
independent of 𝒆 \bm{e} , and use that
𝒆 ′ := 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ​ = 𝑑 ​ 𝒆 , \bm{e}^{\prime}\;:=\;\bm{M}_{i}\,\bm{e}+\beta\,\rho_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\;\overset{d}{=}\;\bm{e}\,,
(A78)
with all first and second moments finite ( π β ∈ 𝒫 2 \pi_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{2} ).
Here = 𝑑 \overset{d}{=} means equality in distribution.
(i) Stationary mean. Taking expectations in
( A78 ) and using the independence of i i and 𝒆 \bm{e}
together with the normal equations E ⁡ [ ρ ​ 𝒂 ] = 𝟎 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho\bm{a}]=\bm{0} :
E ⁡ [ 𝒆 ] = E ⁡ [ 𝒆 ′ ] = E ⁡ [ 𝑴 i ] ​ E ​ [ 𝒆 ] + β ​ E ​ [ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ] = ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ E ​ [ 𝒆 ] , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}^{\prime}]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{M}_{i}\big]\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}]+\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho_{i}\bm{a}_{i}\big]\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}}\big)\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}]\,,
(A79)
hence β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ E ​ [ 𝒆 ] = 𝟎 \beta\bar{\bm{A}}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}]=\bm{0} , and since 𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} is
invertible, E ⁡ [ 𝒆 ] = 𝟎 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}]=\bm{0} , i.e. E π β ​ [ 𝑺 ] = 𝑺 LS \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\bm{S}]=\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} , which holds exactly.
(ii) Exact covariance equation. Expand
𝒆 ′ ​ 𝒆 ′ ⁣ ⊤ \bm{e}^{\prime}\bm{e}^{\prime\top} from ( A78 ):
𝒆 ′ ​ 𝒆 ′ ⁣ ⊤ = 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 ​ 𝒆 ⊤ ​ 𝑴 i + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ + β ​ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒆 ⊤ ​ 𝑴 i + β 2 ​ ρ i 2 ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ . \bm{e}^{\prime}\bm{e}^{\prime\top}\;=\;\bm{M}_{i}\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}\bm{M}_{i}\;+\;\beta\,\rho_{i}\,\bm{M}_{i}\bm{e}\,\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\;+\;\beta\,\rho_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{e}^{\top}\bm{M}_{i}\;+\;\beta^{2}\rho_{i}^{2}\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\,.
(A80)
Take expectations term by term, using the independence of i i and
𝒆 \bm{e} and writing 𝑽 β = E ⁡ [ 𝒆 ​ 𝒆 ⊤ ] \bm{V}_{\beta}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}] .
Homogeneous term. Since
𝑴 i ​ 𝑽 ​ 𝑴 i = 𝑽 − β ⁡ ( 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝑽 + 𝑽 ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ) + β 2 ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝑽 ​ 𝒂 i ) ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ \bm{M}_{i}\bm{V}\bm{M}_{i}=\bm{V}-\beta\big(\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{V}+\bm{V}\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\big)+\beta^{2}\big(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{V}\bm{a}_{i}\big)\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}
(using 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝑽 ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ = ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝑽 ​ 𝒂 i ) ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ \bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{V}\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}=(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{V}\bm{a}_{i})\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top} ),
E ⁡ [ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 ​ 𝒆 ⊤ ​ 𝑴 i ] = 𝑽 β − β ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β + 𝑽 β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ) + β 2 ​ E ​ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{M}_{i}\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}\bm{M}_{i}\big]\;=\;\bm{V}_{\beta}-\beta\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}+\bm{V}_{\beta}\bar{\bm{A}}\big)+\beta^{2}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}\big)\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\,.
(A81)
Cross terms. They are linear in 𝒆 \bm{e} , so independence
factorizes them through E ⁡ [ 𝒆 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}] , which vanishes by part (i):
E ⁡ [ ρ i ​ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ] = E i ​ [ ρ i ​ 𝑴 i ​ E ​ [ 𝒆 ] ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ] = 0 , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho_{i}\,\bm{M}_{i}\bm{e}\,\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\big]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big[\rho_{i}\,\bm{M}_{i}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}]\,\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\big]\;=\;\bm{0}\,,
(A82)
and likewise for its transpose. This is the step where exact
unbiasedness pays off: no O ⁡ ( β 2 ) O(\beta^{2}) cross contribution survives.
Stationarity. Setting E ⁡ [ 𝒆 ′ ​ 𝒆 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ] = 𝑽 β \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}^{\prime}\bm{e}^{\prime\top}]=\bm{V}_{\beta} and
collecting,
𝑽 β = 𝑽 β − β ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β + 𝑽 β ​ 𝑨 ¯ ) + β 2 ​ E ​ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] + β 2 ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] ; \bm{V}_{\beta}\;=\;\bm{V}_{\beta}-\beta\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}+\bm{V}_{\beta}\bar{\bm{A}}\big)+\beta^{2}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}\big)\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]+\beta^{2}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\,;
(A83)
dividing by β \beta gives ( A54 ),
which is an exact equation, not
an expansion.
Trace bounds. Take the trace of ( A54 ):
2 ​ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) = β ​ E ​ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] + β ​ σ 2 . 2\,\tr\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}\big)\;=\;\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}\big)\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\big]\;+\;\beta\,\sigma^{2}\,.
(A84)
Since 𝑽 β ⪰ 𝟎 \bm{V}_{\beta}\succeq\bm{0} , we have 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ≥ 0 \bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}\geq 0 , which gives the lower bound in ( A55 ); and
E ⁡ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] ≤ max i ⁡ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ​ E ​ [ 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ] = max i ⁡ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ​ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a})\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}]\leq\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}]=\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\,\tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}) , so
( 2 − β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) ​ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) ≤ β ​ σ 2 , \big(2-\beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big)\,\tr\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}\big)\;\leq\;\beta\,\sigma^{2}\,,
(A85)
the upper bound (the prefactor is positive by
( A45 )). Finally tr ⁡ 𝑽 β ≤ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) / λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) \tr\bm{V}_{\beta}\leq\tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta})/\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}) gives the Frobenius bound in
( A55 ), and tr ⁡ 𝑽 β = O ⁡ ( β ) \tr\bm{V}_{\beta}=O(\beta) makes the
quartic term in ( A54 ) O ⁡ ( β 2 ) O(\beta^{2}) , leaving the Lyapunov
equation at leading order.
(iii) Misadjustment. For uniform sampling, 𝑨 ¯ = 1 n ​ 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 \bar{\bm{A}}=\tfrac{1}{n}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A} and L ⁡ ( 𝒔 ) = 1 n ​ ‖ 𝑨 ​ 𝒔 − 𝒄 ‖ 2 \mathrm{L}(\bm{s})=\tfrac{1}{n}\left\lVert\bm{A}\bm{s}-\bm{c}\right\rVert^{2} . Decompose with 𝑨 ​ 𝒔 − 𝒄 = 𝑨 ​ 𝒆 − 𝝆 \bm{A}\bm{s}-\bm{c}=\bm{A}\bm{e}-\bm{\rho} , where
𝝆 = ( ρ 1 , … , ρ n ) ⊤ \bm{\rho}=(\rho_{1},\dots,\rho_{n})^{\top} : since 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝝆 = n ​ E ​ [ ρ ​ 𝒂 ] = 𝟎 \bm{A}^{\top}\bm{\rho}=n\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho\bm{a}]=\bm{0} ,
L ⁡ ( 𝒔 ) = 1 n ​ ‖ 𝑨 ​ 𝒆 ‖ 2 − 2 n ​ 𝒆 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝝆 + 1 n ​ ‖ 𝝆 ‖ 2 = 1 n ​ ‖ 𝑨 ​ 𝒆 ‖ 2 + L min , \mathrm{L}(\bm{s})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{n}\left\lVert\bm{A}\bm{e}\right\rVert^{2}-\tfrac{2}{n}\,\bm{e}^{\top}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{\rho}+\tfrac{1}{n}\left\lVert\bm{\rho}\right\rVert^{2}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{n}\left\lVert\bm{A}\bm{e}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathrm{L}_{\min}\,,
(A86)
where L min = 1 n ​ ‖ 𝝆 ‖ 2 \mathrm{L}_{\min}=\tfrac{1}{n}\left\lVert\bm{\rho}\right\rVert^{2} . This also re-proves
that 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} minimizes L \mathrm{L} . Taking stationary expectations,
E π β ​ [ L ] − L min = 1 n ​ E ​ [ 𝒆 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ​ 𝒆 ] = tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\mathrm{L}]-\mathrm{L}_{\min}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{n}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{e}^{\top}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A}\,\bm{e}\big]\;=\;\tr\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\,\bm{V}_{\beta}\big)\,,
(A87)
and inserting the exact trace identity ( A84 ) gives
( A56 ). The two-sided bound
( A57 ) follows from the trace bounds
( A55 ). Since the upper and lower bounds differ by a
factor 2 / ( 2 − β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) = 1 + O ⁡ ( β ) 2/(2-\beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2})=1+O(\beta) , the
excess loss equals β 2 ​ σ 2 ​ ( 1 + O ⁡ ( β ) ) \tfrac{\beta}{2}\sigma^{2}(1+O(\beta)) .
(iv) Transient. Condition on ℱ t \mathcal{F}_{t} and expand
( A49 ):
E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝒆 t + 1 ‖ 2 | ℱ t ] = E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝑴 i ​ 𝒆 t ‖ 2 | ℱ t ] + 2 ​ β ​ E ​ [ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝑴 i ] ​ 𝒆 t + β 2 ​ σ 2 . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{e}_{t+1}\right\rVert^{2}\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{M}_{i}\bm{e}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]\;+\;2\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{M}_{i}\big]\,\bm{e}_{t}\;+\;\beta^{2}\sigma^{2}\,.
(A88)
For the middle term, 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝑴 i = ( 1 − β ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ \bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{M}_{i}=(1-\beta\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2})\,\bm{a}_{i}^{\top} , so by the normal equations
E ⁡ [ ρ i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝑴 i ] = E ​ [ ρ ​ 𝒂 ] ⊤ − β ​ E ​ [ ρ ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ​ 𝒂 ] ⊤ = − β ​ 𝒘 ⊤ , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{M}_{i}\big]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho\,\bm{a}\big]^{\top}-\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\bm{a}\big]^{\top}\;=\;-\,\beta\,\bm{w}^{\top}\,,
(A89)
with 𝒘 = E ⁡ [ ρ ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ​ 𝒂 ] \bm{w}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\bm{a}] as in the statement.
Outside stationarity this cross term does not vanish, and it is
O ⁡ ( β 2 ) O(\beta^{2}) in total. Lemma A1 and Young’s
inequality − 2 ​ β 2 ​ 𝒘 ⊤ ​ 𝒆 ≤ ρ ¯ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒆 ‖ 2 + 2 ​ β 4 ρ ¯ ​ ‖ 𝒘 ‖ 2 -2\beta^{2}\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{e}\leq\tfrac{\bar{\rho}}{2}\left\lVert\bm{e}\right\rVert^{2}+\tfrac{2\beta^{4}}{\bar{\rho}}\left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert^{2} give
E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝒆 t + 1 ‖ 2 | ℱ t ] ≤ ( 1 − ρ ¯ 2 ) ​ ‖ 𝒆 t ‖ 2 + β 2 ​ σ 2 + 2 ​ β 4 ρ ¯ ​ ‖ 𝒘 ‖ 2 . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{e}_{t+1}\right\rVert^{2}\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{t}\big]\;\leq\;\Big(1-\tfrac{\bar{\rho}}{2}\Big)\left\lVert\bm{e}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\;+\;\beta^{2}\sigma^{2}\;+\;\tfrac{2\beta^{4}}{\bar{\rho}}\left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert^{2}\,.
(A90)
Taking full expectations and iterating the affine recursion (its
geometric series is bounded by 2 / ρ ¯ 2/\bar{\rho} ) yields
( A58 ). Since ρ ¯ = γ β ​ λ min = Θ ⁡ ( β ) \bar{\rho}=\gamma_{\beta}\lambda_{\min}=\Theta(\beta) , the horizon 2 ​ β 2 ​ σ 2 / ρ ¯ + 4 ​ β 4 ​ ‖ 𝒘 ‖ 2 / ρ ¯ 2 2\beta^{2}\sigma^{2}/\bar{\rho}+4\beta^{4}\left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert^{2}/\bar{\rho}^{2} is
O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) , consistent with ( A55 ). ∎
Corollary A1 (Recovering exact convergence to 𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ) .
Three standard mechanisms remove the O ⁡ ( β ) O(\sqrt{\beta}) fluctuation:
(i) Diminishing step sizes (Robbins–Monro):
β t ↓ 0 \beta_{t}\downarrow 0 , ∑ t β t = ∞ \sum_{t}\beta_{t}=\infty (e.g. β t ∝ 1 / t \beta_{t}\propto 1/t ) gives 𝑺 t → 𝑺 LS \bm{S}_{t}\to\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} almost
surely.
(ii) Iterate averaging (Polyak–Ruppert / ergodic average):
with constant β \beta , the time average
𝑺 ¯ T = 1 T ​ ∑ t ≤ T 𝑺 t → E π β ​ [ 𝑺 ] = 𝑺 LS \bar{\bm{S}}_{T}=\tfrac{1}{T}\sum_{t\leq T}\bm{S}_{t}\to\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\bm{S}]=\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} almost surely - and by
Theorem A7 (i) the average is exactly unbiased.
(iii) Small- β \beta limit: by ( A55 ),
π β \pi_{\beta} concentrates on 𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} as β → 0 \beta\to 0 , with
the explicit bias–variance trade-off: larger β \beta tracks
nonstationary targets faster, smaller β \beta reduces the
steady-state loss excess ( A57 ), which is the standard
LMS trade-off, inherited verbatim.
Corollary A2 (Consistent targets) .
The inconsistency, not the count n > d 2 n>d^{2} , is what forbids exact
convergence. Let us assume that the targets happen to be realizable, that is
c i = 𝐯 i ⊤ ​ 𝐒 ∘ ​ 𝐤 i c_{i}=\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{S}^{\circ}\bm{k}_{i} for some 𝐒 ∘ \bm{S}^{\circ} .
In this case L min = 0 \mathrm{L}_{\min}=0 , all residuals ρ i \rho_{i} vanish, the driving noise in
( A49 ) disappears, and the Fejér-monotone argument of the
consistent case applies unchanged: 𝐒 t → 𝐒 ∘ \bm{S}_{t}\to\bm{S}^{\circ} (unique by
full column rank) almost surely, linearly, for arbitrarily many
associations. Two distinguished targets realize this structurally:
(i) the correlation target c i = 𝒗 i ⊤ ​ 𝒌 i c_{i}=\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{k}_{i} , solved by
𝑺 = 𝑰 \bm{S}=\bm{I} for every n n ;
(ii) the norm target c i = ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ 2 c_{i}=\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2} , solved by any
exact retrieval memory 𝑺 ​ 𝒌 i = 𝒗 i \bm{S}\bm{k}_{i}=\bm{v}_{i} (in particular
autoassociatively by 𝑺 = 𝑰 \bm{S}=\bm{I} ).
For these targets the recognition memory has effectively unbounded association count as long as realizability holds.
A3.5.5 Remarks
Memory for the overloaded setting.
Beyond capacity, 𝑺 \bm{S} is no longer a table but an online linear
regressor : the read-out 𝒗 ⊤ ​ 𝑺 LS ​ 𝒌 \bm{v}^{\top}\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\bm{k} is the best
linear prediction (in the p p -weighted least-squares sense) of the
scalar signal c c from the bilinear feature 𝒌 ⊗ 𝒗 \bm{k}\otimes\bm{v} .
Degradation is graceful since overload produces averaging plus an
O ⁡ ( β ) O(\sqrt{\beta}) jitter, never blow-up, since the homogeneous factors
keep spectral norm ≤ 1 \leq 1 .
Assumptions for the proofs.
Independence of i t i_{t} from the past makes the mean recursion exact
and factorizes the covariance cross terms; the normal equations kill
the driving term in the mean and the cross terms in stationarity
(via E ⁡ [ 𝒆 ] = 𝟎 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{e}]=\bm{0} ); the step condition ( A45 )
enters three times: (1) γ β > 0 \gamma_{\beta}>0 (contraction),
(2) β ​ λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) < 2 \beta\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})<2 (mean stability), and
(3) 2 − β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 > 0 2-\beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}>0 (trace upper bound).
Full column rank gives λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) > 0 \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})>0 , used for
uniqueness of 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} , for ρ ¯ > 0 \bar{\rho}>0 , and to convert
tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) \tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}) into a bound on tr ⁡ 𝑽 β \tr\bm{V}_{\beta} . Linearity
of the update in the state is what makes every step of the argument
exact rather than a Taylor expansion which we obtain from the quadratic
loss.
Conflicting targets on the same pair: averaging vs. overwriting.
The canonical inconsistency is one pair ( 𝒌 , 𝒗 ) (\bm{k},\bm{v}) presented with
different targets. Under random sampling with probabilities
p j p_{j} over targets c j c_{j} , the stationary mean read-out is the weighted
average, 𝒗 ⊤ ​ E π β ​ [ 𝑺 ] ​ 𝒌 = ∑ j p j ​ c j / ∑ j p j \bm{v}^{\top}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\bm{S}]\,\bm{k}=\sum_{j}p_{j}c_{j}/\sum_{j}p_{j} (the LS solution for identical rows). Under sequential presentation the same rule overwrites : the
contraction drives the read-out to the most recent target. Random
replay averages; ordered replay recencies, therefore the sampling protocol,
not the rule, decides about overwrite behavior.
Forget gate: recency-weighted least squares.
With the scalar gated variant
𝑺 t + 1 = f t ​ 𝑺 t − β ⁡ ( 𝒗 t ⊤ ​ f t ​ 𝑺 t ​ 𝒌 t − c t ) ​ 𝒗 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ \bm{S}_{t+1}=\mathrm{f}_{t}\bm{S}_{t}-\beta(\bm{v}_{t}^{\top}\mathrm{f}_{t}\bm{S}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}-c_{t})\bm{v}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top} , f t ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) \mathrm{f}_{t}\in(0,1) , the steady state is the
corresponding exponentially weighted least-squares solution:
old associations are geometrically down-weighted, so the effective
association count is finite ( ∼ 1 / ( 1 − f ) \sim 1/(1-\mathrm{f}) ) and the memory tracks
a drifting target distribution. Therefore, the overloaded regime is exactly
where the forget gate is most relevant.
Summary.
For n > d 2 n>d^{2} associations the randomized Kronecker Delta Rule with
constant β \beta does not converge to a point but to a stationary
distribution: its mean is exactly the sampling-weighted
least-squares memory 𝑺 LS = argmin ∑ i p i ​ ( 𝒗 i ⊤ ​ 𝑺 ​ 𝒌 i − c i ) 2 \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}\sum_{i}p_{i}(\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{k}_{i}-c_{i})^{2} (the minimizer of L \mathrm{L} for uniform sampling), its
covariance is O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) and solves the exact stationarity equation
( A54 ) driven by the optimal residuals, and its loss sits at
L min + β 2 ​ E ​ [ ρ i 2 ​ ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ 2 ] \mathrm{L}_{\min}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho_{i}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}] up to an explicit 1 + O ⁡ ( β ) 1+O(\beta) factor. All of
this is proven from first principles: an exact linear mean
recursion with spectral radius < 1 <1 ; a synchronous-coupling
𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} -contraction plus Banach fixed point for existence,
uniqueness, and geometric convergence of π β \pi_{\beta} ; exact moment
identities in stationarity for mean and covariance; an exact trace
identity for the misadjustment; and a Young-inequality recursion for
the transient. Averaging or annealing β \beta recovers
𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} exactly; consistent target structures (correlation
target, norm target with a retrieval solution) escape the capacity
limit altogether; and a forget gate turns the steady state into
recency-weighted least squares.
A3.6 Norm Bounds for the KronLSTM with Steady-State Solution
We derive a two-sided norm bound for the steady state of the randomized
Kronecker Delta Rule with more than d 2 d^{2} associations.
Running memories or data stream i.e. online updates
are covered via the steady state, therefore we obtain an expected bound
on the online updated memory.
A3.6.1 Overview
For the overloaded KronLSTM ( n n associations, features
𝒂 i = 𝒌 i ⊗ 𝒗 i \bm{a}_{i}=\bm{k}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i} , sampling weights p i p_{i} ) the
randomized Kronecker Delta Rule converges around the weighted
least-squares memory 𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} . We derive a two-sided
bound on its Frobenius norm from an exact energy (Pythagoras)
identity:
E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ ‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) , \frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]}{\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\;\leq\;\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,,
where ρ i \rho_{i} are the optimal residuals and
𝑨 ¯ = E ⁡ [ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] \bar{\bm{A}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}] is the feature second moment. The
numerator is the explained target energy ; the bound is
attained (both sides coincide) for isotropic designs, and in the
orthonormal design it reproduces
‖ 𝑺 ⋆ ‖ F 2 = ∑ i c i 2 \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\sum_{i}c_{i}^{2} of the target-weighted
Hebbian superposition exactly. Combined with the steady-state
analysis, it yields an a-priori bound on the expected memory norm of
the running iterates.
A3.6.2 Setting and nomenclature
As before, we assume n n associations ( 𝒌 i , 𝒗 i , c i ) (\bm{k}_{i},\bm{v}_{i},c_{i}) with features
𝒂 i = 𝒌 i ⊗ 𝒗 i ∈ ℝ d 2 \bm{a}_{i}=\bm{k}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} ,
‖ 𝒂 i ‖ = ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ \left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert , sampling probabilities
p i > 0 p_{i}>0 , and
𝑨 ¯ \displaystyle\bar{\bm{A}}\;
= E ⁡ [ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ , 𝒔 LS = argmin 𝒔 E ⁡ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝒔 − c ) 2 ] = 𝑨 ¯ − 1 ​ E ​ [ c ​ 𝒂 ] , \displaystyle=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\,,\qquad\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\;=\;\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}_{\bm{s}}\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{s}-c)^{2}\big]\;=\;\bar{\bm{A}}^{-1}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c\,\bm{a}\big]\,,
(A91)
ρ i \displaystyle\rho_{i}\;
= c i − 𝒂 i ⊤ 𝒔 LS , E [ ρ 𝒂 ] = 0 (normal equations) , \displaystyle=\;c_{i}-\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\,,\qquad\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho\,\bm{a}\big]\;=\;\bm{0}\quad\text{(normal equations)}\,,
(A92)
assuming 𝑨 ¯ ≻ 𝟎 \bar{\bm{A}}\succ\bm{0} (features span ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} ; the
rank-deficient case is treated in the remarks). All expectations are
over i ∼ p i\sim p . Recall
‖ 𝒔 ‖ 2 = ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F \left\lVert\bm{s}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} for 𝒔 = vec ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) \bm{s}=\vecop(\bm{S}) , and that
for uniform sampling E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] = L min \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]=\mathrm{L}_{\min} , the minimum of the
loss L \mathrm{L} .
A3.6.3 An exact energy identity
Lemma A3 (Energy identity) .
Let y i := 𝐚 i ⊤ ​ 𝐬 LS = 𝐯 i ⊤ ​ 𝐒 LS ​ 𝐤 i y_{i}:=\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\bm{v}_{i}^{\top}\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\bm{k}_{i}
be the optimal read-outs. Then E ⁡ [ ρ ​ y ] = 0 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho\,y]=0 and
E ⁡ [ c 2 ] = E ⁡ [ y 2 ] + E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] , ( 𝒔 LS ) ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS = E ⁡ [ y 2 ] = E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c^{2}\big]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[y^{2}\big]\;+\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\big]\,,\qquad\big(\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\big)^{\!\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\;\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[y^{2}\big]\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c^{2}\big]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\big]\,.
(A93)
Proof.
E ⁡ [ ρ ​ y ] = E ​ [ ρ ​ 𝒂 ] ⊤ ​ 𝒔 LS = 0 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho\,y]=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho\,\bm{a}]^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=0 by the
normal equations; with c = y + ρ c=y+\rho this gives the first identity.
The second follows from
E ⁡ [ y 2 ] = ( 𝒔 LS ) ⊤ ​ E ​ [ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] ​ 𝒔 LS \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[y^{2}]=(\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}})^{\top}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}]\,\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} .
∎
The stored read-out energy of the least-squares memory equals
the target energy minus the unexplained (residual) energy. Therefore, the
memory can only be as large as the part of the target signal that the
bilinear features can explain.
A3.6.4 The Norm Bound
Theorem A8 (Two-sided Frobenius-norm bound) .
With λ min , λ max \lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max} the extreme eigenvalues of
𝐀 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} ,
E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ ‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \boxed{\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c^{2}\big]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\big]}{\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\;\leq\;\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c^{2}\big]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\big]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,.\;}
(A94)
In particular, since
σ max ​ ( 𝐒 LS ) ≤ ‖ 𝐒 LS ‖ F \sigma_{\max}(\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}})\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} ,
σ max ​ ( 𝑺 LS ) ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \sigma_{\max}\big(\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\big)\;\leq\;\sqrt{\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c^{2}\big]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\big]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}}\;\leq\;\sqrt{\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c^{2}\big]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}}\,.
(A95)
Proof.
Sandwich the quadratic form:
λ min ​ ‖ 𝒔 LS ‖ 2 ≤ ( 𝒔 LS ) ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS ≤ λ max ​ ‖ 𝒔 LS ‖ 2 \lambda_{\min}\left\lVert\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert^{2}\leq(\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}})^{\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\,\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\leq\lambda_{\max}\left\lVert\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert^{2} , and insert
( A93 ).
∎
Corollary A3 (First-moment variant) .
From λ min ​ ‖ 𝐬 ‖ 2 ≤ 𝐬 ⊤ ​ 𝐀 ¯ ​ 𝐬 = 𝐬 ⊤ ​ E ​ [ c ​ 𝐚 ] ≤ ‖ 𝐬 ‖ ​ E ​ [ | c | ​ ‖ 𝐚 ‖ ] \lambda_{\min}\left\lVert\bm{s}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\bm{s}^{\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{s}=\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c\bm{a}]\leq\left\lVert\bm{s}\right\rVert\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[|c|\,\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert] ,
‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F ≤ E ⁡ [ | c i | ​ ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\,|c_{i}|\,\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert\big]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,.
(A96)
The accumulated write mass
β ​ c t ​ ‖ 𝐤 t ‖ ​ ‖ 𝐯 t ‖ \beta\,c_{t}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert is exactly the quantity whose
first moment appears here. The second moment bound
( A94 ) is usually much sharper (it divides by
λ min \lambda_{\min} only once, under the square root).
A3.6.5 Tightness and special cases
Isotropic design gives equality.
If 𝑨 ¯ = E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] d 2 ​ 𝑰 \bar{\bm{A}}=\tfrac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}]}{d^{2}}\,\bm{I}
(features spread evenly over ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} ), then
λ min = λ max \lambda_{\min}=\lambda_{\max} and ( A94 ) collapses to
the identity
‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 = d 2 ​ ( E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] ) E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] . \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;=\;\frac{d^{2}\,\big(\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]\big)}{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}]}\,.
(A97)
Orthonormal design recovers the Hebbian superposition
norm.
For n = d 2 n=d^{2} with orthonormal { 𝒌 i } \{\bm{k}_{i}\} , orthonormal { 𝒗 i } \{\bm{v}_{i}\} ,
and uniform sampling, the features are orthonormal,
𝑨 ¯ = 𝑰 / d 2 \bar{\bm{A}}=\bm{I}/d^{2} , the system is consistent ( ρ ≡ 0 \rho\equiv 0 ),
and ( A94 ) holds with equality:
‖ 𝑺 ⋆ ‖ F 2 = d 2 ​ E ​ [ c 2 ] = ∑ i = 1 d 2 c i 2 , \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;=\;d^{2}\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{d^{2}}c_{i}^{2}\,,
(A98)
exactly the Frobenius norm of the target-weighted Hebbian
superposition 𝑺 ⋆ = ∑ i c i ​ 𝒗 i ​ 𝒌 i ⊤ \bm{S}^{\star}=\sum_{i}c_{i}\bm{v}_{i}\bm{k}_{i}^{\top} . The bound is
therefore attained, not merely an estimate.
Unit-norm keys and values give a d 2 d^{2} conditioning factor.
If ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ = ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ = 1 \left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert=1 then tr ⁡ 𝑨 ¯ = E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] = 1 \tr\bar{\bm{A}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}]=1 , so λ min ≤ 1 / d 2 ≤ λ max \lambda_{\min}\leq 1/d^{2}\leq\lambda_{\max} : the lower bound in ( A94 ) is at most
E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}] , the upper bound at least
d 2 ​ ( E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] ) d^{2}(\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]) , with both collapsing to the latter
in the isotropic case. Ill-spread designs pay in λ min \lambda_{\min} :
the worst case is a target profile aligned with the least-covered
feature direction, and the 1 / λ min 1/\lambda_{\min} dependence is
unavoidable in general.
Consistent case where association errors are zero.
If the system is consistent ( ρ ≡ 0 \rho\equiv 0 ; e.g. n ≤ d 2 n\leq d^{2} with independent features, or the correlation/norm
targets), then 𝑺 LS = 𝑺 ⋆ \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\bm{S}^{\star} is the exact solution and
‖ 𝑺 ⋆ ‖ F 2 ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,.
(A99)
Residual energy can only shrink the memory: overload
(inconsistency) makes the least-squares memory smaller than a
consistent memory with the same target energy, never larger.
Rank-deficient features.
If the features do not span ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} ( n < d 2 n<d^{2} or dependent
𝒂 i \bm{a}_{i} ), replace 𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} by the minimum-Frobenius-norm
least-squares solution and λ min \lambda_{\min} by the smallest nonzero eigenvalue of 𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} : since
𝒔 LS ∈ span ⁡ { 𝒂 i } \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\in\spanop\{\bm{a}_{i}\} (as do the iterates from
𝑺 0 = 𝟎 \bm{S}_{0}=\bm{0} ), the sandwich argument applies on that subspace and
( A94 ) holds verbatim with λ min + \lambda_{\min}^{+} .
A3.6.6 Bound on the memory norm for steady-state and all updates
Bound on the memory norm for steady-state distribution.
First, we consider a bound on the memory norm under
the steady-state distribution.
Corollary A4 (Bound on the steady-state memory norm) .
Under the randomized rule with step size
0 < β < 2 / max i ⁡ ‖ 𝐚 i ‖ 2 0<\beta<2/\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2} , the stationary distribution
π β \pi_{\beta} has mean exactly 𝐒 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} , so the second moment
decomposes orthogonally,
E π β ​ ‖ 𝐒 ‖ F 2 = ‖ 𝐒 LS ‖ F 2 + tr ⁡ 𝐕 β \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\tr\bm{V}_{\beta} , and with the covariance trace bound of the
steady-state note,
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) + β ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒌 ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 ] ( 2 − β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\!\left[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\;+\;\frac{\beta\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\left\lVert\bm{k}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}\big]}{\big(2-\beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big)\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,.
(A100)
The first term is the size of the memory content, the second the
O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) fluctuation; for β → 0 \beta\to 0 or in the consistent case
the bound reduces to Theorem A8 .
Theorem A9 (Two-sided memory norm bound for the steady state) .
Let π β \pi_{\beta} be the steady-state law of ( A16 ),
𝐕 β = E π β ​ [ 𝐞 ​ 𝐞 ⊤ ] \bm{V}_{\beta}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}] its covariance as in
Theorem A7 (ii) (the two coincide because the
stationary mean is exactly 𝐬 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ), σ 2 = E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ​ ‖ 𝐚 ‖ 2 ] \sigma^{2}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}] ,
and write
A max 2 := max i ⁡ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 = max i ⁡ ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ 2 , κ := β ​ A max 2 ∈ ( 0 , 2 ) , A_{\max}^{2}\;:=\;\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\;=\;\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\,,\qquad\kappa\;:=\;\beta\,A_{\max}^{2}\;\in\;(0,2)\,,
(A101)
the last inclusion being the step-size condition
( A45 ). Then the energy identity
𝒔 LS ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS = E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\,\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]\,-\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]
(A102)
holds, hence the least-squares memory is pinned on both sides,
E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ ‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) , \frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]}{\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\;\leq\;\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,,
(A103)
and the steady-state memory itself satisfies
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] = ‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 + tr ⁡ 𝑽 β , tr ⁡ 𝑽 β ≤ β ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] ( 2 − κ ) ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;=\;\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;+\;\tr\bm{V}_{\beta}\,,\qquad\tr\bm{V}_{\beta}\;\leq\;\frac{\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\big]}{\big(2-\kappa\big)\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,.
(A104)
The fluctuation term admits the exact decomposition
tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) \displaystyle\tr\big(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}\big)\;
= β 2 ​ σ 2 + R β , \displaystyle=\;\frac{\beta}{2}\,\sigma^{2}\;+\;R_{\beta}\,,
(A105)
R β \displaystyle R_{\beta}\;
: = β 2 ​ E ​ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] , 0 ≤ R β ≤ κ 2 − κ ⋅ β ​ σ 2 2 . \displaystyle:=\;\frac{\beta}{2}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}\big)\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\big]\,,\qquad 0\;\leq\;R_{\beta}\;\leq\;\frac{\kappa}{2-\kappa}\cdot\frac{\beta\,\sigma^{2}}{2}\,.
R β R_{\beta} is bounded by
β 2 ​ A max 2 ​ σ 2 / ( 2 ​ ( 2 − κ ) ) \beta^{2}A_{\max}^{2}\sigma^{2}/\big(2(2-\kappa)\big) .
Consequently, since E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}] ,
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] ≤ 1 λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ ( E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − 2 ​ ( 1 − κ ) 2 − κ ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ] ) ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ max ⁡ { 1 , κ 2 − κ } , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\left(\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]\;-\;\frac{2\,(1-\kappa)}{2-\kappa}\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]\right)\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\;\max\!\left\{1,\;\frac{\kappa}{2-\kappa}\right\}\,,
(A106)
and in particular, whenever the step size satisfies
β ​ ‖ 𝐚 t ‖ 2 ≤ 1 \beta\left\lVert\bm{a}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 1 (i.e. κ ≤ 1 \kappa\leq 1 , the
non-expansive regime),
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \boxed{\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,.\;}
(A107)
All statements are exact and non-asymptotic: they hold for every
β \beta admitted by ( A45 ).
Proof.
The normal equations 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS = 𝒃 \bar{\bm{A}}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\bm{b} give
E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] = E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − 2 ​ 𝒃 ⊤ ​ 𝒔 LS + 𝒔 LS ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS = E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − 𝒔 LS ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-2\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}+\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} , which is ( A102 ); bounding the Rayleigh
quotient 𝒔 LS ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} between
λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ ‖ 𝒔 LS ‖ 2 \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\left\lVert\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert^{2} and
λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ ‖ 𝒔 LS ‖ 2 \lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})\left\lVert\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert^{2} , and using
‖ 𝒔 ‖ 2 = ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F \left\lVert\bm{s}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} , gives ( A103 ). The
first part of ( A104 ) is the bias–variance split
E ​ ‖ 𝒔 ‖ 2 = ‖ E ​ 𝒔 ‖ 2 + TrCov ⁡ ( 𝒔 ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{s}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\bm{s}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\mathbf{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{s}) together with
the fact that the stationary mean is exactly 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}
(Theorem A7 (i)).
Exact stationarity equation. Rather than the leading-order
Lyapunov equation, we use the exact stationarity equation
( A54 ) of Theorem A7 (ii),
𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β + 𝑽 β ​ 𝑨 ¯ − β ​ E ​ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] = β ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] , \bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}+\bm{V}_{\beta}\bar{\bm{A}}\;-\;\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\,\bm{a}\big)\,\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\;=\;\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\,\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\,,
(A108)
whose trace is
2 ​ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) = β ​ σ 2 + β ​ E ​ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] 2\tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta})=\beta\sigma^{2}+\beta\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a})\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}] . This is ( A105 ), and it
identifies the remainder: R β ≥ 0 R_{\beta}\geq 0 because
𝑽 β ⪰ 𝟎 \bm{V}_{\beta}\succeq\bm{0} , while
E ⁡ [ ( 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ) ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] ≤ A max 2 ​ E ​ [ 𝒂 ⊤ ​ 𝑽 β ​ 𝒂 ] = A max 2 ​ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a})\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}]\leq A_{\max}^{2}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{V}_{\beta}\bm{a}]=A_{\max}^{2}\tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta})
gives
( 2 − κ ) ​ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) ≤ β ​ σ 2 \big(2-\kappa\big)\tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta})\leq\beta\sigma^{2} ,
the prefactor being positive by ( A45 ). Substituting
this bound back into R β = β ​ A max 2 2 ​ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) R_{\beta}=\tfrac{\beta A_{\max}^{2}}{2}\tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}) ’s upper estimate yields the stated bound on
R β R_{\beta} , and
λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ tr ⁡ 𝑽 β ≤ tr ⁡ ( 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝑽 β ) \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\tr\bm{V}_{\beta}\leq\tr(\bar{\bm{A}}\bm{V}_{\beta}) yields the second part of ( A104 ).
Deriving the clean bound. 𝑺 = 𝟎 \bm{S}=\bm{0} is feasible, so
E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}] ; moreover
σ 2 = E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] ≤ A max 2 ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ] \sigma^{2}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}]\leq A_{\max}^{2}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}] ,
hence β ​ σ 2 ≤ κ ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ] \beta\sigma^{2}\leq\kappa\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}] and
tr ⁡ 𝑽 β ≤ κ 2 − κ ⋅ E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) . \tr\bm{V}_{\beta}\;\leq\;\frac{\kappa}{2-\kappa}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,.
(A109)
Adding this to ( A103 ) gives
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] ≤ 1 λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ​ ( E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] + κ 2 − κ ​ E ​ [ ρ 2 ] ) , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\left(\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]+\frac{\kappa}{2-\kappa}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]\right),
(A110)
and − 1 + κ / ( 2 − κ ) = − 2 ( 1 − κ ) / ( 2 − κ ) -1+\kappa/(2-\kappa)=-2(1-\kappa)/(2-\kappa) is the
