coefficient in ( A106 ). For κ ≤ 1 \kappa\leq 1 that coefficient
is ≤ 0 \leq 0 , so dropping the E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}] term gives
( A107 ); for κ > 1 \kappa>1 , using
E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}]\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}] gives the factor
1 + 2 ​ ( κ − 1 ) / ( 2 − κ ) = κ / ( 2 − κ ) 1+2(\kappa-1)/(2-\kappa)=\kappa/(2-\kappa) . ∎
Residuals lower the bound.
The E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}] term in ( A106 ) enters with a negative sign throughout the non-expansive regime κ ≤ 1 \kappa\leq 1 :
overload shrinks the bound but does not inflate it, because residual
energy is energy the bilinear features cannot explain and therefore
cannot store. The two effects of ρ \rho are less memory content
( A103 ) and more steady-state jitter
( A104 ). They cancel exactly at κ = 1 \kappa=1 and the
content term dominates below it. This is why the constant in
( A107 ) is 1 1 rather than the 3 / 2 3/2 or 2 2 obtained by
bounding the two contributions separately.
Bound on the memory norm for all updates.
Next, we consider the bound even before the memory
𝑺 t \bm{S}_{t} reaches the steady state but is in the update phase.
Theorem A10 (The bound applies to the running or online memory at
every step: not only in the steady state) .
Let μ t := law ​ ( 𝐬 t ) = μ 0 ​ P t ∈ 𝒫 2 \mu_{t}:=\text{law}(\bm{s}_{t})=\mu_{0}P^{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{2} be the law of
the iterate at step t t under ( A16 ), so that π β \pi_{\beta} is
the unique fixed point of the same kernel, and set
q β := 1 − β ⁡ ( 2 − κ ) ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) . q_{\beta}\;:=\;1\;-\;\beta\,\big(2-\kappa\big)\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\;\in\;[0,1)\,.
(A111)
Started at 𝐒 0 = 𝟎 \bm{S}_{0}=\bm{0} , the running memory obeys, for every t t and
in the non-expansive regime κ ≤ 1 \kappa\leq 1 ,
E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝑺 t ‖ F 2 ] ≤ ( 1 + q β t / 2 ) 2 ​ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ 4 ​ E ​ [ c 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) , \boxed{\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;\leq\;\Big(1+q_{\beta}^{\,t/2}\Big)^{2}\,\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\;\leq\;\frac{4\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,,\;}
(A112)
where the transient factor expands as
( 1 + q β t / 2 ) 2 = 1 + 2 ​ q β t / 2 + q β t ↓ 1 \big(1+q_{\beta}^{\,t/2}\big)^{2}=1+2q_{\beta}^{\,t/2}+q_{\beta}^{t}\downarrow 1 , so the bound decays geometrically from 4 4 to the
steady-state constant 1 1 of ( A107 ).
For a general step size κ ∈ ( 0 , 2 ) \kappa\in(0,2) the same statement holds
with E ⁡ [ c 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}] replaced by
E ⁡ [ c 2 ] ​ max ⁡ { 1 , κ / ( 2 − κ ) } \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]\max\{1,\kappa/(2-\kappa)\} ; for a general deterministic
start 𝐒 0 \bm{S}_{0} it reads
E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝑺 t ‖ F 2 ] ≤ ( B β + q β t / 2 ​ ‖ 𝑺 0 − 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 + tr ⁡ 𝑽 β ) 2 , B β := E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] 1 / 2 . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;\leq\;\left(B_{\beta}\;+\;q_{\beta}^{\,t/2}\sqrt{\left\lVert\bm{S}_{0}-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\tr\bm{V}_{\beta}}\,\right)^{\!2},\qquad B_{\beta}:=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]^{1/2}.
(A113)
Nothing on the right-hand side depends on the sequence length: the
expected squared memory norm never exceeds four times its
steady-state bound from the very first step, and
relaxes geometrically to that bound.
Proof.
Rate. Couple two copies of ( A16 ) synchronously,
i.e. drive them with the same ( 𝒂 t , c t ) (\bm{a}_{t},c_{t}) . The additive term
cancels in the difference 𝒅 t \bm{d}_{t} , leaving
𝒅 t + 1 = ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝒂 t ​ 𝒂 t ⊤ ) ​ 𝒅 t \bm{d}_{t+1}=(\bm{I}-\beta\bm{a}_{t}\bm{a}_{t}^{\top})\bm{d}_{t} , and
E ⁡ [ ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ) 2 ] = 𝑰 − 2 ​ β ​ 𝑨 ¯ + β 2 ​ E ​ [ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] ⪯ 𝑰 − β ⁡ ( 2 − κ ) ​ 𝑨 ¯ , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[(\bm{I}-\beta\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top})^{2}\big]\;=\;\bm{I}-2\beta\bar{\bm{A}}+\beta^{2}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\;\preceq\;\bm{I}-\beta\big(2-\kappa\big)\bar{\bm{A}}\,,
(A114)
using β 2 ​ E ​ [ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ​ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] ⪯ β ​ κ ​ 𝑨 ¯ \beta^{2}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}]\preceq\beta\kappa\bar{\bm{A}} . Hence
E ​ ‖ 𝒅 t + 1 ‖ 2 ≤ q β ​ E ​ ‖ 𝒅 t ‖ 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{d}_{t+1}\right\rVert^{2}\leq q_{\beta}\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{d}_{t}\right\rVert^{2} , and
taking the infimum over couplings gives
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ t , π β ) ≤ q β t / 2 ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ 0 , π β ) \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{t},\pi_{\beta})\leq q_{\beta}^{t/2}\,\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{0},\pi_{\beta}) . (Equivalently q β = 1 − ρ ¯ q_{\beta}=1-\bar{\rho} with the
ρ ¯ = γ β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) \bar{\rho}=\gamma_{\beta}\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}) of
Lemma A1 , since γ β ≥ β ⁡ ( 2 − κ ) \gamma_{\beta}\geq\beta(2-\kappa) ;
q β q_{\beta} is written out here only to keep the statement
self-contained.) Since 𝒔 ↦ ‖ 𝒔 ‖ \bm{s}\mapsto\left\lVert\bm{s}\right\rVert
is 1 1 -Lipschitz, the reverse triangle inequality in L 2 L^{2} gives
E ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 t ‖ F 2 ] 1 / 2 ≤ B β + 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ t , π β ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}]^{1/2}\leq B_{\beta}+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{t},\pi_{\beta}) .
The initial term is not an estimate. For a deterministic start
the only coupling of μ 0 = δ 𝑺 0 \mu_{0}=\delta_{\bm{S}_{0}} with π β \pi_{\beta} is the
product, so the infimum defining 𝒲 2 \mathcal{W}_{2} is attained and
𝒲 2 ​ ( μ 0 , π β ) 2 = E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 − 𝑺 0 ‖ F 2 ] = ‖ 𝑺 0 − 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 + tr ⁡ 𝑽 β , \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{0},\pi_{\beta})^{2}\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}-\bm{S}_{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;=\;\left\lVert\bm{S}_{0}-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;+\;\tr\bm{V}_{\beta}\,,
(A115)
the second equality being the bias–variance split around the
stationary mean 𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} (Theorem A7 (i)). At
𝑺 0 = 𝟎 \bm{S}_{0}=\bm{0} the right-hand side is exactly
‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 + tr ⁡ 𝑽 β = B β 2 \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\tr\bm{V}_{\beta}=B_{\beta}^{2} by
( A104 ), i.e. 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ 0 , π β ) = B β \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{0},\pi_{\beta})=B_{\beta} :
the distance from the empty memory to the steady state is the
steady-state size. The two terms therefore collapse into one,
E ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 t ‖ F 2 ] 1 / 2 ≤ ( 1 + q β t / 2 ) ​ B β , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]^{1/2}\;\leq\;\big(1+q_{\beta}^{\,t/2}\big)\,B_{\beta}\,,
(A116)
and squaring and inserting
B β 2 ≤ E ⁡ [ c 2 ] / λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) B_{\beta}^{2}\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]/\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}) from
( A107 ) gives ( A112 ).
Squaring preserves the inequality since both sides are nonnegative.
Finally
q β t / 2 ≤ 1 q_{\beta}^{\,t/2}\leq 1 bounds the transient factor by 4 4 . The
general- κ \kappa variant replaces B β 2 B_{\beta}^{2} by the bound of
( A106 ), and the general- 𝑺 0 \bm{S}_{0} variant
( A113 ) keeps 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ 0 , π β ) \mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{0},\pi_{\beta}) in the form
( A115 ) instead of collapsing it. ∎
Summary.
The least-squares memory satisfies the exact energy identity
( 𝒔 LS ) ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ¯ ​ 𝒔 LS = E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] (\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}})^{\top}\bar{\bm{A}}\,\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}] , which sandwiches its Frobenius norm between
( E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] ) / λ max (\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}])/\lambda_{\max} and
( E ⁡ [ c 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ] ) / λ min (\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho^{2}])/\lambda_{\min} . This is the explained target
energy divided by the extreme feature-covariance eigenvalues. The
bound is an equality for isotropic designs, reproduces
∑ i c i 2 \sum_{i}c_{i}^{2} in the orthonormal design, extends to the running
iterates with an additive O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) fluctuation term, shrinks under
overload since the residual energy is subtracted.
Appendix A4 DeltaNet Steady-State Norm Bound
A4.1 Overview
The DeltaNet steady-state norm bound can be derived
essentially verbatim to kronLSTM. DeltaNet’s update is affine in
𝑺 t \bm{S}_{t} and acts on each row of the memory independently;
each row obeys exactly the scalar delta-rule recursion analyzed for
KronLSTM, with the association direction 𝒂 t = 𝒌 t ⊗ 𝒗 t \bm{a}_{t}=\bm{k}_{t}\otimes\bm{v}_{t} replaced by the key 𝒌 t \bm{k}_{t} and the scalar target c t c_{t}
replaced by the value coordinate v t , j v_{t,j} . Every step of the
argument — the energy identity, the two-sided bound, the exact
stationary covariance bound, and the Wasserstein contraction that
carries all of it to the running memory — therefore transfers, and
yields
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] ≤ 2 ​ E ​ [ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 ] / λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}]\leq 2\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}]/\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}}) with 𝑲 ¯ = E ⁡ [ 𝒌 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ] \bar{\bm{K}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\top}] .
The practical consequence is a scoping correction: a
length-independent steady-state bound is a property of the delta-rule mechanism , shared by KronLSTM and DeltaNet, which
separates both from mLSTM and from any stability argument resting
on a forget gate. We also record where the two genuinely differ: in the
step-size condition, in what the bound is a bound on , and in
the conditioning constant that sets its scale.
A4.2 DeltaNet Affine Recursion
DeltaNet’s update is
𝑺 t + 1 = 𝑺 t − β t ​ ( 𝑺 t ​ 𝒌 t − 𝒗 t ) ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ = 𝑺 t ​ ( 𝑰 − β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ ) + β t ​ 𝒗 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ , \bm{S}_{t+1}\;=\;\bm{S}_{t}\;-\;\beta_{t}\big(\bm{S}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}-\bm{v}_{t}\big)\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\;=\;\bm{S}_{t}\big(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\big)\;+\;\beta_{t}\,\bm{v}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\,,
(A117)
and Gated DeltaNet’s is the same with 𝑺 t \bm{S}_{t} replaced by
f t ​ 𝑺 t \mathrm{f}_{t}\bm{S}_{t} in the first term. Two features are immediate. First,
( A117 ) is affine in 𝑺 t \bm{S}_{t} , so the pair
(memory, random input) generates a Markov chain of exactly the type
analyzed for the Kronecker Delta Rule. Second, and decisively, the
memory is acted on from the right only, so the rows of 𝑺 \bm{S} do
not mix. Transposing ( A117 ),
𝑺 t + 1 ⊤ = ( 𝑰 − β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ ) ​ 𝑺 t ⊤ + β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒗 t ⊤ , \bm{S}_{t+1}^{\top}\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\big)\bm{S}_{t}^{\top}\;+\;\beta_{t}\,\bm{k}_{t}\bm{v}_{t}^{\top}\,,
(A118)
and reading off the j j -th column, i.e. the j j -th row
𝒔 ( j ) := 𝑺 ⊤ ​ 𝒆 j \bm{s}^{(j)}:=\bm{S}^{\top}\bm{e}_{j} of 𝑺 \bm{S} ,
𝒔 t + 1 ( j ) = ( 𝑰 − β t 𝒌 t 𝒌 t ⊤ ) 𝒔 t ( j ) + β t v t , j 𝒌 t , j = 1 , … , d . \bm{s}^{(j)}_{t+1}\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\big)\,\bm{s}^{(j)}_{t}\;+\;\beta_{t}\,v_{t,j}\,\bm{k}_{t}\,,\qquad j=1,\dots,d\,.
(A119)
The structural correspondence.
Equation ( A119 ) is identical to the vectorized
KronLSTM recursion
𝒔 t + 1 = ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝒂 t ​ 𝒂 t ⊤ ) ​ 𝒔 t + β ​ c t ​ 𝒂 t \bm{s}_{t+1}=(\bm{I}-\beta\bm{a}_{t}\bm{a}_{t}^{\top})\bm{s}_{t}+\beta c_{t}\bm{a}_{t}
under the substitution
𝒂 t = 𝒌 t ⊗ 𝒗 t ⟼ 𝒌 t , c t ⟼ v t , j , ℝ d 2 ⟼ ℝ d . \bm{a}_{t}\;=\;\bm{k}_{t}\otimes\bm{v}_{t}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\bm{k}_{t}\,,\qquad c_{t}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;v_{t,j}\,,\qquad\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\mathbb{R}^{d}\,.
(A120)
DeltaNet is thus d d independent copies of the same scalar
delta rule all driven by a common
key 𝒌 t \bm{k}_{t} and differing only in their scalar targets v t , j v_{t,j} .
KronLSTM is a single copy of that rule on a d 2 d^{2} -dimensional state
with the richer direction 𝒌 t ⊗ 𝒗 t \bm{k}_{t}\otimes\bm{v}_{t} . Neither is a
generalization of the other; both are Kaczmarz/LMS iterations, which
is why the same theory covers both.
A4.3 Steady-State Norm Bound
Model the stream as i.i.d. triples ( 𝒌 t , 𝒗 t , β t ) ∼ p (\bm{k}_{t},\bm{v}_{t},\beta_{t})\sim p
and put
𝑲 ¯ \displaystyle\bar{\bm{K}}\;
: = E ⁡ [ 𝒌 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ] ≻ 0 , 𝑺 LS := E ⁡ [ 𝒗 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ] ​ 𝑲 ¯ − 1 = argmin 𝑺 E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝑺 ​ 𝒌 − 𝒗 ‖ 2 ] , \displaystyle:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\top}\big]\;\succ\;\bm{0}\,,\qquad\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\;:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{v}\bm{k}^{\top}\big]\,\bar{\bm{K}}^{-1}\;=\;\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}_{\bm{S}}\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\bm{k}-\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}\big]\,,
(A121)
𝝆 \displaystyle\bm{\rho}\;
: = 𝒗 − 𝑺 LS ​ 𝒌 , \displaystyle:=\;\bm{v}-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\bm{k}\,,
so that E ⁡ [ 𝝆 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ] = 𝟎 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{\rho}\bm{k}^{\top}]=\bm{0} . With a random, input-dependent
β t \beta_{t} the same statements hold with 𝑲 ¯ \bar{\bm{K}} replaced by the
β \beta -weighted second moment E ⁡ [ β ​ 𝒌 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\beta\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\top}] and
𝑺 LS \bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} by the correspondingly weighted least-squares
solution; nothing else changes. Assume the step size satisfies
β t ​ ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ≤ 1 \beta_{t}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 1 , which for Gated DeltaNet’s
sigmoid-bounded β t ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) \beta_{t}\in(0,1) and unit-norm keys holds by
construction.
Proposition A1 (Two-sided steady-state norm bound for DeltaNet) .
Let π β \pi_{\beta} be the steady-state law of ( A117 ) and
𝚺 β \bm{\Sigma}_{\beta} the covariance of vec ⁡ ( 𝐒 ) \vecop(\bm{S}) under it. Then the
energy identity
Tr ⁡ ( 𝑺 LS ​ 𝑲 ¯ ​ 𝑺 LS ⊤ ) = E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 ] − E ⁡ [ ‖ 𝝆 ‖ 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\big(\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\bar{\bm{K}}\,\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}\top}\big)\;=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}\big]\,-\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{\rho}\right\rVert^{2}\big]
(A122)
holds, hence
E ​ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 − E ​ ‖ 𝝆 ‖ 2 λ max ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ≤ ‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 ≤ E ​ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 − E ​ ‖ 𝝆 ‖ 2 λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) , \frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{\rho}\right\rVert^{2}}{\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{K}})}\;\leq\;\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}-\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{\rho}\right\rVert^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})}\,,
(A123)
and the steady-state memory satisfies, exactly and without any
expansion in β \beta ,
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] = ‖ 𝑺 LS ‖ F 2 + Tr ​ 𝚺 β , Tr ​ 𝚺 β ≤ β ​ E ​ [ ‖ 𝝆 ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒌 ‖ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;=\;\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\;+\;\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\beta}\,,\qquad\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\beta}\;\leq\;\frac{\beta\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{\rho}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{k}\right\rVert^{2}\big]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})}\,,
(A124)
so that in particular
E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 ] ≤ 2 ​ E ​ [ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]\;\leq\;\frac{2\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}\big]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})}\,.
(A125)
Proof.
Apply the KronLSTM argument to ( A119 ) for each j j and
sum. The normal equations 𝑲 ¯ ​ 𝒔 ( j ) , LS = E ⁡ [ v j ​ 𝒌 ] \bar{\bm{K}}\bm{s}^{(j),\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[v_{j}\bm{k}]
give E ⁡ [ ρ j 2 ] = E ⁡ [ v j 2 ] − 𝒔 ( j ) , LS ⊤ ​ 𝑲 ¯ ​ 𝒔 ( j ) , LS \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\rho_{j}^{2}]=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[v_{j}^{2}]-\bm{s}^{(j),\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}\top}\bar{\bm{K}}\bm{s}^{(j),\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ; summing over j j and using
∑ j 𝒔 ( j ) ⊤ ​ 𝑲 ¯ ​ 𝒔 ( j ) = Tr ⁡ ( 𝑺 ​ 𝑲 ¯ ​ 𝑺 ⊤ ) \sum_{j}\bm{s}^{(j)\top}\bar{\bm{K}}\bm{s}^{(j)}=\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}(\bm{S}\bar{\bm{K}}\bm{S}^{\top}) yields ( A122 ). Bounding each summand’s Rayleigh
quotient between λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ​ ‖ 𝒔 ( j ) ‖ 2 \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})\left\lVert\bm{s}^{(j)}\right\rVert^{2} and
λ max ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ​ ‖ 𝒔 ( j ) ‖ 2 \lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{K}})\left\lVert\bm{s}^{(j)}\right\rVert^{2} and summing, together
with ∑ j ‖ 𝒔 ( j ) ‖ 2 = ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F 2 \sum_{j}\left\lVert\bm{s}^{(j)}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2} , gives
( A123 ). For ( A124 ), write
𝑬 := 𝑺 − 𝑺 LS \bm{E}:=\bm{S}-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} , so that ( A117 ) becomes
𝑬 t + 1 = 𝑬 t ​ ( 𝑰 − β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ ) + β t ​ 𝝆 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ \bm{E}_{t+1}=\bm{E}_{t}(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top})+\beta_{t}\bm{\rho}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top} with ( 𝒌 t , 𝝆 t ) (\bm{k}_{t},\bm{\rho}_{t}) independent of 𝑬 t \bm{E}_{t} . Taking
squared Frobenius norms in stationarity, the cross term vanishes
because E ⁡ [ 𝑬 t ] = 𝟎 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{E}_{t}]=\bm{0} , leaving

(A126)
Since β ​ ‖ 𝒌 ‖ 2 ≤ 1 \beta\left\lVert\bm{k}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 1 ,

(A127)
and cancelling E ​ ‖ 𝑬 ‖ F 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{E}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2} from both sides gives

Finally 𝑺 = 𝟎 \bm{S}=\bm{0} is feasible, so
E ​ ‖ 𝝆 ‖ 2 ≤ E ​ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{\rho}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2} , which bounds both terms of
( A124 ) by E ​ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 / λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}/\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})
and gives ( A125 ).
∎
Corollary A5 (Transfer to the running or online memory) .
With μ t \mu_{t} the law of 𝐒 t \bm{S}_{t} and
B β := E π β ​ [ ‖ 𝐒 ‖ F 2 ] 1 / 2 B_{\beta}:=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi_{\beta}}[\left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}]^{1/2} ,
E ​ [ ‖ 𝑺 t ‖ F 2 ] 1 / 2 ≤ B β + ( 1 − β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ) t / 2 ​ 𝒲 2 ​ ( μ 0 , π β ) , B β 2 ≤ 2 ​ E ​ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]^{1/2}\;\leq\;B_{\beta}\;+\;\big(1-\beta\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})\big)^{t/2}\,\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{0},\pi_{\beta})\,,\qquad B_{\beta}^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{2\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})}\,.
(A128)
Proof.
Synchronous coupling: driving two copies with the same
( 𝒌 t , 𝒗 t , β t ) (\bm{k}_{t},\bm{v}_{t},\beta_{t}) cancels the additive term, leaving
𝑫 t + 1 = 𝑫 t ​ ( 𝑰 − β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ ) \bm{D}_{t+1}=\bm{D}_{t}(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}) , whence
E ​ ‖ 𝑫 t + 1 ‖ F 2 ≤ ( 1 − β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ) ​ E ​ ‖ 𝑫 t ‖ F 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{D}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq(1-\beta\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}}))\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{D}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2} by the
same positive-semidefinite inequality as above. Take the infimum over
couplings and use that ‖ ⋅ ‖ F \left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} is 1 1 -Lipschitz.
∎
A4.4 Relation to the KronLSTM Bound
The bound is a consequence of the delta rule being a relaxed
projection that is a correction proportional to a residual that shrinks as
the memory improves. Both models have that structure,
therefore separates the delta-rule family from mLSTM, whose Hebbian
write is uncorrected and has no fixed point to contract toward.
It separates the steady-state argument from the
non-expansiveness argument , whose forget-gate bound
β ​ C ​ A max / ( 1 − f ) \beta CA_{\max}/(1-\mathrm{f}) is Ω ⁡ ( T ) \Omega(T) once f → 1 \mathrm{f}\to 1 as long
contexts require.
The constants are not comparable.
The two bounds read
KronLSTM: E ⁡ [ c 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) , DeltaNet: 2 ​ E ​ [ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 ] λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) , 𝑨 ¯ = E [ 𝒂 𝒂 ⊤ ] ∈ ℝ d 2 × d 2 , 𝑲 ¯ ∈ ℝ d × d . \text{KronLSTM:}\;\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\,,\qquad\text{DeltaNet:}\;\;\frac{2\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2}]}{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})}\,,\qquad\bar{\bm{A}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times d^{2}},\;\;\bar{\bm{K}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\,.
(A129)
These are bounds on the same object, ‖ 𝑺 ‖ F \left\lVert\bm{S}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} , but they are
not bounds for the same task: KronLSTM’s stream carries one scalar
c t c_{t} per step, DeltaNet’s a d d -vector 𝒗 t \bm{v}_{t} , so E ⁡ [ c 2 ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[c^{2}] and
E ​ ‖ 𝒗 ‖ 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert^{2} measure different amounts of information being
written. The denominators also differ structurally:
𝑲 ¯ ≻ 𝟎 \bar{\bm{K}}\succ\bm{0} requires only that the keys span ℝ d \mathbb{R}^{d} ,
whereas 𝑨 ¯ ≻ 𝟎 \bar{\bm{A}}\succ\bm{0} requires the 𝒂 i = 𝒌 i ⊗ 𝒗 i \bm{a}_{i}=\bm{k}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i} to span ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} , and λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}) is
typically the smaller of the two. The reason is that for independent isotropic keys
and values, 𝑨 ¯ = E ⁡ [ 𝒌 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ] ⊗ E ⁡ [ 𝒗 ​ 𝒗 ⊤ ] \bar{\bm{A}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\top}]\otimes\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}]
gives λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) = λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ​ λ min ​ ( E ⁡ [ 𝒗 ​ 𝒗 ⊤ ] ) \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})=\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})\,\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}]) , a product of two small numbers. On the other hand
the constant 2 appears in the bound of the DeltaNet. Which
constant is smaller in a given setting is an empirical question, not
a structural one.
DeltaNet has a smaller bound.
The step-size condition is milder. KronLSTM requires
β ​ ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 t ‖ 2 ≤ 1 \beta\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 1 , so a large value norm
eats directly into the admissible step size and hence into the
contraction rate β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) \beta\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}) ; DeltaNet requires
only β t ​ ‖ 𝒌 t ‖ 2 ≤ 1 \beta_{t}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 1 , in which 𝒗 t \bm{v}_{t} does not appear
at all. It enters the drive but never the stability condition.
KronLSTM has advantages.
d 2 d^{2} rather than d d exactly satisfiable associations
per scalar slot, interference gated by the product of key and
value overlap rather than key overlap alone, an update direction
𝒗 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ \bm{v}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top} fixed by the input pair rather than the
state-dependent 𝒖 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ \bm{u}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top} so that a write’s footprint on
unrelated memories does not compound with context length,
single-binding write/rewrite/erase, and linearity of the memory in
the target sequence. None of these follow from, or are affected by,
the steady-state bound.
Appendix A5 Retention Rates for KronLSTM and DeltaNet
A5.1 KronLSTM Setting
Let n > d 2 n>d^{2} associations ( 𝒌 i , 𝒗 i , c i ) (\bm{k}_{i},\bm{v}_{i},c_{i}) , 1 ≤ i ≤ n 1\leq i\leq n , be
given. Vectorized, with 𝒔 = vec ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) ∈ ℝ d 2 \bm{s}=\vecop(\bm{S})\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} and
𝒂 i = 𝒌 i ⊗ 𝒗 i \bm{a}_{i}=\bm{k}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}
( ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ = ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ \left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert ), the associations form
the linear system 𝑨 ​ 𝒔 = 𝒄 \bm{A}\bm{s}=\bm{c} with 𝑨 ∈ ℝ n × d 2 \bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d^{2}}
stacking the rows 𝒂 i ⊤ \bm{a}_{i}^{\top} ; we assume 𝑨 \bm{A} has full column rank
d 2 d^{2} (the features span ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} ). At each step an index i t i_{t}
is drawn with probabilities p i > 0 p_{i}>0 , independently of the past, and
the rule is applied with fixed step size β \beta satisfying
0 < β ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 < 2 for all ​ i . 0\;<\;\beta\,\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\;<\;2\qquad\text{for all }i\,.
(A130)
Define
𝑨 ¯ \displaystyle\bar{\bm{A}}\;
: = E ⁡ [ 𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ ≻ 0 , \displaystyle:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\;\succ\;\bm{0}\,,
𝒃 ¯ \displaystyle\bar{\bm{b}}\;
: = E ⁡ [ c ​ 𝒂 ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ c i ​ 𝒂 i , \displaystyle:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[c\,\bm{a}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,c_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\,,
(A131)
𝒔 LS \displaystyle\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\;
: = 𝑨 ¯ − 1 ​ 𝒃 ¯ = argmin 𝒔 ∑ i = 1 n p i ​ ( 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒔 − c i ) 2 , \displaystyle:=\;\bar{\bm{A}}^{-1}\bar{\bm{b}}\;=\;\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}\,}_{\bm{s}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\big(\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}-c_{i}\big)^{2}\,,
ρ i \displaystyle\rho_{i}\;
: = c i − 𝒂 i ⊤ 𝒔 LS (optimal residuals) , \displaystyle:=\;c_{i}-\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\quad\text{(optimal residuals)}\,,
(A132)
so that the normal equations read
E [ ρ 𝒂 ] = ∑ i = 1 n p i ρ i 𝒂 i = 0 ⟺ 𝒃 ¯ = 𝑨 ¯ 𝒔 LS . \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho\,\bm{a}\big]\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\,\rho_{i}\,\bm{a}_{i}\;=\;\bm{0}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\bar{\bm{b}}\;=\;\bar{\bm{A}}\,\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\,.
(A133)
For uniform sampling, 𝑨 ¯ = 1 n ​ 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 \bar{\bm{A}}=\tfrac{1}{n}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A} and
𝒔 LS = ( 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝑨 ) − 1 ​ 𝑨 ⊤ ​ 𝒄 \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A})^{-1}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{c} is the ordinary
least-squares solution, i.e. the unique minimizer of L \mathrm{L} .
Throughout, expectations without subscript are over i ∼ p i\sim p , and
E π \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\pi} denotes expectation of a random state distributed
according to a law π \pi . Vectorized, the rule and its error form are
𝒔 t + 1 = 𝑴 i t ​ 𝒔 t + β ​ c i t ​ 𝒂 i t , 𝒆 t + 1 = 𝑴 i t ​ 𝒆 t + β ​ ρ i t ​ 𝒂 i t , 𝑴 i := 𝑰 − β ​ 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ , \bm{s}_{t+1}\;=\;\bm{M}_{i_{t}}\bm{s}_{t}+\beta\,c_{i_{t}}\bm{a}_{i_{t}}\,,\qquad\bm{e}_{t+1}\;=\;\bm{M}_{i_{t}}\,\bm{e}_{t}\;+\;\beta\,\rho_{i_{t}}\,\bm{a}_{i_{t}}\,,\qquad\bm{M}_{i}\;:=\;\bm{I}-\beta\,\bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\,,
(A134)
with 𝒆 t := 𝒔 t − 𝒔 LS \bm{e}_{t}:=\bm{s}_{t}-\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ; the error form follows by
inserting c i = 𝒂 i ⊤ ​ 𝒔 LS + ρ i c_{i}=\bm{a}_{i}^{\top}\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}+\rho_{i} . The constants
γ β := min i ⁡ β ⁡ ( 2 − β ​ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 ) > 0 , ρ ¯ := γ β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] , σ 2 := E ⁡ [ ρ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒂 ‖ 2 ] \gamma_{\beta}\;:=\;\min_{i}\,\beta\,\big(2-\beta\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\big)\;>\;0\,,\qquad\bar{\rho}\;:=\;\gamma_{\beta}\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\;\in\;(0,1]\,,\qquad\sigma^{2}\;:=\;\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\rho^{2}\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\big]
(A135)
satisfy 0 < ρ ¯ ≤ 1 0<\bar{\rho}\leq 1 .
Under ( A130 ), the expected iterate obeys the exact
deterministic recursion
E [ 𝒔 t + 1 ] = ( 𝑰 − β 𝑨 ¯ ) E [ 𝒔 t ] + β 𝒃 ¯ ⟺ E [ 𝒆 t + 1 ] = ( 𝑰 − β 𝑨 ¯ ) E [ 𝒆 t ] , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{s}_{t+1}\big]\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}}\big)\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{s}_{t}\big]\;+\;\beta\,\bar{\bm{b}}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{e}_{t+1}\big]\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}}\big)\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{e}_{t}\big]\,,
(A136)
whose unique fixed point is 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} , and
‖ E ⁡ [ 𝑺 t ] − 𝑺 LS ‖ F ≤ r t ​ ‖ 𝑺 0 − 𝑺 LS ‖ F , r := max k ⁡ | 1 − β ​ λ k ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) | < 1 , \left\lVert\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{S}_{t}]-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\;\leq\;r^{t}\,\left\lVert\bm{S}_{0}-\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\,,\qquad r\;:=\;\max_{k}\,{{\left|1-\beta\lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{A}})\right|}}\;<\;1\,,
(A137)
so E ⁡ [ 𝑺 t ] → 𝑺 LS \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{S}_{t}]\to\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} linearly. The convergence of the
mean is exactly unbiased : the fixed point of ( A136 )
is 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} itself, with no O ⁡ ( β ) O(\beta) correction.
A5.2 DeltaNet Setting
DeltaNet’s update is
𝑺 t + 1 = 𝑺 t − β t ​ ( 𝑺 t ​ 𝒌 t − 𝒗 t ) ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ = 𝑺 t ​ ( 𝑰 − β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ ) + β t ​ 𝒗 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ , \bm{S}_{t+1}\;=\;\bm{S}_{t}\;-\;\beta_{t}\big(\bm{S}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}-\bm{v}_{t}\big)\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\;=\;\bm{S}_{t}\big(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\big)\;+\;\beta_{t}\,\bm{v}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\,,
(A138)
and Gated DeltaNet’s is the same with 𝑺 t \bm{S}_{t} replaced by
f t ​ 𝑺 t \mathrm{f}_{t}\bm{S}_{t} in the first term. Two features are immediate. First,
( A138 ) is affine in 𝑺 t \bm{S}_{t} , so the pair
(memory, random input) generates a Markov chain of exactly the type
analyzed for the Kronecker Delta Rule. Second, and decisively, the
memory is acted on from the right only, so the rows of 𝑺 \bm{S} do
not mix. Transposing ( A138 ),
𝑺 t + 1 ⊤ = ( 𝑰 − β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒌 t ⊤ ) ​ 𝑺 t ⊤ + β t ​ 𝒌 t ​ 𝒗 t ⊤ , \bm{S}_{t+1}^{\top}\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\big)\bm{S}_{t}^{\top}\;+\;\beta_{t}\,\bm{k}_{t}\bm{v}_{t}^{\top}\,,
(A139)
and reading off the j j -th column, i.e. the j j -th row
𝒔 ( j ) := 𝑺 ⊤ ​ 𝒆 j \bm{s}^{(j)}:=\bm{S}^{\top}\bm{e}_{j} of 𝑺 \bm{S} ,
𝒔 t + 1 ( j ) = ( 𝑰 − β t 𝒌 t 𝒌 t ⊤ ) 𝒔 t ( j ) + β t v t , j 𝒌 t , j = 1 , … , d . \bm{s}^{(j)}_{t+1}\;=\;\big(\bm{I}-\beta_{t}\bm{k}_{t}\bm{k}_{t}^{\top}\big)\,\bm{s}^{(j)}_{t}\;+\;\beta_{t}\,v_{t,j}\,\bm{k}_{t}\,,\qquad j=1,\dots,d\,.
(A140)
We will use later the key covariance matrix 𝑲 ¯ = E ⁡ [ 𝒌 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ] \bar{\bm{K}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\top}] and the value covariance matrix 𝑽 ¯ = E ⁡ [ 𝒗 ​ 𝒗 ⊤ ] \bar{\bm{V}}=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}] .
The structural correspondence.
Equation ( A140 ) is identical to the vectorized
KronLSTM recursion
𝒔 t + 1 = ( 𝑰 − β ​ 𝒂 t ​ 𝒂 t ⊤ ) ​ 𝒔 t + β ​ c t ​ 𝒂 t \bm{s}_{t+1}=(\bm{I}-\beta\bm{a}_{t}\bm{a}_{t}^{\top})\bm{s}_{t}+\beta c_{t}\bm{a}_{t}
under the substitution
𝒂 t = 𝒌 t ⊗ 𝒗 t ⟼ 𝒌 t , c t ⟼ v t , j , ℝ d 2 ⟼ ℝ d . \bm{a}_{t}\;=\;\bm{k}_{t}\otimes\bm{v}_{t}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\bm{k}_{t}\,,\qquad c_{t}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;v_{t,j}\,,\qquad\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\mathbb{R}^{d}\,.
(A141)
DeltaNet is thus d d independent copies of the same scalar
delta rule all driven by a common
key 𝒌 t \bm{k}_{t} and differing only in their scalar targets v t , j v_{t,j} .
KronLSTM is a single copy of that rule on a d 2 d^{2} -dimensional state
with the richer direction 𝒌 t ⊗ 𝒗 t \bm{k}_{t}\otimes\bm{v}_{t} . Neither is a
generalization of the other; both are Kaczmarz/LMS iterations, which
is why the same theory covers both.
A5.3 KronLSTM Retention of the Initial Memory
We consider KronLSTM and the Kronecker Delta Rule.
We ask how much of an initial memory 𝒔 0 \bm{s}_{0} survives after t t steps.
The mean recursion ( A136 ) is affine, so it separates the
two contributions exactly. Writing 𝑴 := 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ \bm{M}:=\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}} and
unrolling,
E ⁡ [ 𝒔 t ] = 𝑴 t ​ 𝒔 0 + β ​ ∑ τ = 0 t − 1 𝑴 τ ​ 𝒃 ¯ = 𝑴 t ​ 𝒔 0 ⏟ initial memory + ( 𝑰 − 𝑴 t ) ​ 𝒔 LS ⏟ acquired memory , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}\big[\bm{s}_{t}\big]\;=\;\bm{M}^{t}\bm{s}_{0}\;+\;\beta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\bm{M}^{\tau}\,\bar{\bm{b}}\;=\;\underbrace{\bm{M}^{t}\,\bm{s}_{0}}_{\text{initial memory}}\;+\;\underbrace{\big(\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{t}\big)\,\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}}_{\text{acquired memory}}\,,
(A142)
where the geometric sum was evaluated with
β ​ ∑ τ = 0 t − 1 𝑴 τ = ( 𝑰 − 𝑴 t ) ​ ( 𝑰 − 𝑴 ) − 1 ​ β = ( 𝑰 − 𝑴 t ) ​ 𝑨 ¯ − 1 \beta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\bm{M}^{\tau}=(\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{t})(\bm{I}-\bm{M})^{-1}\beta=(\bm{I}-\bm{M}^{t})\bar{\bm{A}}^{-1} and 𝑨 ¯ − 1 ​ 𝒃 ¯ = 𝒔 LS \bar{\bm{A}}^{-1}\bar{\bm{b}}=\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} ; equivalently
E ⁡ [ 𝒔 t ] − 𝒔 LS = 𝑴 t ​ ( 𝒔 0 − 𝒔 LS ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{s}_{t}]-\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}=\bm{M}^{t}(\bm{s}_{0}-\bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}) , which is
the error recursion ( A134 ) in the mean.
Everything in E ⁡ [ 𝒔 t ] \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{s}_{t}] that depends on 𝒔 0 \bm{s}_{0} sits in the first
term, and it depends on it linearly. The natural measure of retention
is therefore the read-out of that term along the stored direction,
ℛ t ​ ( 𝒔 0 ) := 𝒔 0 ⊤ ​ ∂ E ⁡ [ 𝒔 t ] ∂ 𝒔 0 ​ 𝒔 0 = 𝒔 0 ⊤ ​ 𝑴 t ​ 𝒔 0 , ‖ 𝒔 0 ‖ = 1 , \mathcal{R}_{t}(\bm{s}_{0})\;:=\;\bm{s}_{0}^{\top}\frac{\partial\,\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{s}_{t}]}{\partial\,\bm{s}_{0}}\,\bm{s}_{0}\;=\;\bm{s}_{0}^{\top}\bm{M}^{t}\,\bm{s}_{0}\,,\qquad\left\lVert\bm{s}_{0}\right\rVert=1\,,
(A143)
the fraction of a unit initial memory still legible after t t steps.
It equals 1 1 at t = 0 t=0 and requires no assumption relating 𝒔 0 \bm{s}_{0} to
𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} : the acquired term is filtered out by differentiation
rather than by orthogonality.
Proposition A2 (Retention rate) .
Assume κ := β ​ A max 2 ≤ 1 \kappa:=\beta\,A_{\max}^{2}\leq 1 with
A max 2 = max i ⁡ ‖ 𝐚 i ‖ 2 A_{\max}^{2}=\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2} , so that
β ​ λ max ​ ( 𝐀 ¯ ) ≤ 1 \beta\,\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})\leq 1 and 𝐌 ⪰ 𝟎 \bm{M}\succeq\bm{0} . Then
for every unit 𝐬 0 \bm{s}_{0}
( 1 − β ​ λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ) t ≤ ℛ t ​ ( 𝒔 0 ) ≤ ( 1 − β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ) t , \big(1-\beta\,\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})\big)^{t}\;\;\leq\;\;\mathcal{R}_{t}(\bm{s}_{0})\;\;\leq\;\;\big(1-\beta\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\big)^{t}\,,
(A144)
both bounds being attained, at the corresponding eigenvectors of
𝐀 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} . Averaged over a uniformly random unit 𝐬 0 \bm{s}_{0} the
retention is exactly the normalized trace,
E 𝒔 0 ​ [ ℛ t ​ ( 𝒔 0 ) ] = Tr ​ 𝑴 t d 2 = 1 d 2 ​ ∑ k = 1 d 2 ( 1 − β ​ λ k ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ) t , \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\bm{s}_{0}}\big[\mathcal{R}_{t}(\bm{s}_{0})\big]\;=\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\,\bm{M}^{t}}{d^{2}}\;=\;\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{d^{2}}\big(1-\beta\,\lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{A}})\big)^{t}\,,
(A145)
and the retention horizon — the number of steps after which the
slowest mode has decayed to 1 / e 1/\mathrm{e} — is
t 1 / e = 1 / log ⁡ ( 1 − β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝐀 ¯ ) ) − 1 ≃ 1 / ( β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝐀 ¯ ) ) t_{1/\mathrm{e}}=1/\log\big(1-\beta\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\big)^{-1}\simeq 1/\big(\beta\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\big) .
Proof.
𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} is symmetric, so 𝑴 = 𝑰 − β ​ 𝑨 ¯ \bm{M}=\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{A}} is symmetric
with eigenvalues 1 − β ​ λ k ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) 1-\beta\lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{A}})\in[0,1) under
β ​ λ max ≤ 1 \beta\lambda_{\max}\leq 1 , hence 𝑴 t \bm{M}^{t} is symmetric positive
semidefinite with eigenvalues ( 1 − β ​ λ k ) t (1-\beta\lambda_{k})^{t} .
Equation ( A144 ) is the Rayleigh-quotient bound for
𝑴 t \bm{M}^{t} , with equality on its extreme eigenvectors, which are those of
𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} for λ max \lambda_{\max} and λ min \lambda_{\min} respectively.
For ( A145 ), E ⁡ [ 𝒔 0 ​ 𝒔 0 ⊤ ] = 𝑰 / d 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{s}_{0}\bm{s}_{0}^{\top}]=\bm{I}/d^{2} for a
uniform unit vector in ℝ d 2 \mathbb{R}^{d^{2}} , so
E ⁡ [ 𝒔 0 ⊤ ​ 𝑴 t ​ 𝒔 0 ] = Tr ⁡ ( 𝑴 t ​ E ​ [ 𝒔 0 ​ 𝒔 0 ⊤ ] ) = Tr ⁡ ( 𝑴 t ) / d 2 \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{s}_{0}^{\top}\bm{M}^{t}\bm{s}_{0}]=\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}(\bm{M}^{t}\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{s}_{0}\bm{s}_{0}^{\top}])=\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}(\bm{M}^{t})/d^{2} .
The step-size condition holds because
λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ max i ⁡ ‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 = A max 2 \lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})\leq\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}=A_{\max}^{2} ,
𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} being a convex combination of the 𝒂 i ​ 𝒂 i ⊤ \bm{a}_{i}\bm{a}_{i}^{\top} .
∎
A5.4 DeltaNet Retention of the Initial Memory
Corollary A6 (DeltaNet retention) .
By the row decomposition ( A140 ), DeltaNet’s mean memory
obeys E ⁡ [ 𝐒 t ] = 𝐒 0 ​ 𝐍 t + 𝐒 LS ​ ( 𝐈 − 𝐍 t ) \mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{S}_{t}]=\bm{S}_{0}\bm{N}^{t}+\bm{S}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}}(\bm{I}-\bm{N}^{t}) with
𝐍 := 𝐈 − β ​ 𝐊 ¯ \bm{N}:=\bm{I}-\beta\bar{\bm{K}} , so with
ℛ t ​ ( 𝐒 0 ) := ⟨ 𝐒 0 , 𝐒 0 ​ 𝐍 t ⟩ F \mathcal{R}_{t}(\bm{S}_{0}):={{\left\langle\bm{S}_{0},\bm{S}_{0}\bm{N}^{t}\right\rangle}}_{\mathrm{F}} and
‖ 𝐒 0 ‖ F = 1 \left\lVert\bm{S}_{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}=1 ,
( 1 − β ​ λ max ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ) t ≤ ℛ t ​ ( 𝑺 0 ) ≤ ( 1 − β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ) t , E 𝑺 0 ​ [ ℛ t ] = Tr ​ 𝑵 t d , \big(1-\beta\,\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{K}})\big)^{t}\;\;\leq\;\;\mathcal{R}_{t}(\bm{S}_{0})\;\;\leq\;\;\big(1-\beta\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})\big)^{t}\,,\qquad\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}_{\bm{S}_{0}}\big[\mathcal{R}_{t}\big]\;=\;\frac{\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\,\bm{N}^{t}}{d}\,,
(A146)
under β ​ max i ​ ‖ 𝐤 i ‖ 2 ≤ 1 \beta\max_{i}\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 1 . The statement is
Proposition A2 under the substitution
𝐀 ¯ ↦ 𝐊 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}}\mapsto\bar{\bm{K}} , d 2 ↦ d d^{2}\mapsto d of
Section A4.2 .
A5.5 KronLSTM Retains Longer than DeltaNet
The two retention rates are governed by the spectra of 𝑨 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} and
𝑲 ¯ \bar{\bm{K}} , and for independent keys and values these are related
exactly.
Proposition A3 (Spectral comparison) .
Let 𝐤 \bm{k} and 𝐯 \bm{v} be independent and let
𝐕 ¯ := E ⁡ [ 𝐯 ​ 𝐯 ⊤ ] \bar{\bm{V}}:=\mathbf{\mathrm{E}}[\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}] . Then
𝐀 ¯ = 𝐊 ¯ ⊗ 𝐕 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}}=\bar{\bm{K}}\otimes\bar{\bm{V}} , so the eigenvalues of
𝐀 ¯ \bar{\bm{A}} are the d 2 d^{2} pairwise products of those of 𝐊 ¯ \bar{\bm{K}} and
𝐕 ¯ \bar{\bm{V}} , and in particular
λ min ​ ( 𝐀 ¯ ) = λ min ​ ( 𝐊 ¯ ) ​ λ min ​ ( 𝐕 ¯ ) \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})=\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{V}}) and
λ max ​ ( 𝐀 ¯ ) = λ max ​ ( 𝐊 ¯ ) ​ λ max ​ ( 𝐕 ¯ ) \lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})=\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{K}})\,\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{V}}) . If the values are ℓ 2 \ell_{2} -normalized,
‖ 𝐯 ‖ = 1 \left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert=1 , then Tr ​ 𝐕 ¯ = 1 \mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\bar{\bm{V}}=1 and hence
λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) d , λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ λ max ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) , \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})\;\;\leq\;\;\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})}{d}\,,\qquad\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})\;\;\leq\;\;\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{K}})\,,
(A147)
with equality in the first when the values are isotropic,
𝐕 ¯ = 𝐈 / d \bar{\bm{V}}=\bm{I}/d .
Proof.
𝒂 ​ 𝒂 ⊤ = ( 𝒌 ⊗ 𝒗 ) ​ ( 𝒌 ⊗ 𝒗 ) ⊤ = 𝒌 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ⊗ 𝒗 ​ 𝒗 ⊤ \bm{a}\bm{a}^{\top}=(\bm{k}\otimes\bm{v})(\bm{k}\otimes\bm{v})^{\top}=\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\top}\otimes\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top} by the mixed-product rule, and
independence factorizes the expectation. The spectrum of a Kronecker
product of symmetric matrices is the set of pairwise products.
For normalized values λ min ​ ( 𝑽 ¯ ) ≤ Tr ​ 𝑽 ¯ / d = 1 / d \lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{V}})\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\bar{\bm{V}}/d=1/d and λ max ​ ( 𝑽 ¯ ) ≤ Tr ​ 𝑽 ¯ = 1 \lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{V}})\leq\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\bar{\bm{V}}=1 , giving
( A147 ); isotropy forces 𝑽 ¯ = 𝑰 / d \bar{\bm{V}}=\bm{I}/d and the first
inequality becomes an equality.
∎
Because keys and values are ℓ 2 \ell_{2} -normalized in the KronLSTM block,
‖ 𝒂 i ‖ 2 = ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ 2 ​ ‖ 𝒗 i ‖ 2 = 1 = ‖ 𝒌 i ‖ 2 \left\lVert\bm{a}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}=1=\left\lVert\bm{k}_{i}\right\rVert^{2} , so both models admit exactly the same step sizes
β ≤ 1 \beta\leq 1 and the comparison at equal β \beta is well posed. Under
Propositions A2 and A3 :
•
The slowest mode decays d d times more slowly. KronLSTM’s
worst-case retention ( 1 − β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ) t (1-\beta\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}}))^{t} dominates
DeltaNet’s ( 1 − β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ) t (1-\beta\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}}))^{t} , and the retention
horizons are
t 1 / e KronLSTM ≃ 1 β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≥ d β ​ λ min ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) = d ​ t 1 / e DeltaNet , t_{1/\mathrm{e}}^{\text{KronLSTM}}\;\simeq\;\frac{1}{\beta\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{A}})}\;\;\geq\;\;\frac{d}{\beta\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{K}})}\;=\;d\;t_{1/\mathrm{e}}^{\text{DeltaNet}}\,,
(A148)
a factor d d in context length at which a stored association is still
legible, with equality for isotropic values.
•
No mode decays faster. By the second half of
( A147 ), λ max ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) ≤ λ max ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) \lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{A}})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\bm{K}}) , so even KronLSTM’s fastest-decaying mode is
no faster than DeltaNet’s. The comparison is therefore not a trade of
some directions against others: the whole spectrum of retention rates
moves in KronLSTM’s favour. For isotropic values it moves by exactly
the factor 1 / d 1/d , λ k ​ ( 𝑨 ¯ ) = λ k ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) / d \lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{A}})=\lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{K}})/d with
multiplicity d d each.
•
On average, likewise. Comparing ( A145 ) with
( A146 ) for isotropic values,
Tr ​ 𝑴 t / d 2 = 1 d ​ ∑ k ( 1 − β ​ λ k ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) / d ) t ≥ 1 d ​ ∑ k ( 1 − β ​ λ k ​ ( 𝑲 ¯ ) ) t = Tr ​ 𝑵 t / d \mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\bm{M}^{t}/d^{2}=\tfrac{1}{d}\sum_{k}(1-\beta\lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{K}})/d)^{t}\geq\tfrac{1}{d}\sum_{k}(1-\beta\lambda_{k}(\bar{\bm{K}}))^{t}=\mathbf{\mathrm{Tr}}\bm{N}^{t}/d termwise.
Retention is bought from adaptation.
The same matrix 𝑴 t \bm{M}^{t} governs both terms of ( A142 ): the
acquired memory approaches 𝒔 LS \bm{s}^{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{S}} at rate 𝑰 − 𝑴 t \bm{I}-\bm{M}^{t} .
Retaining the initial memory d d times longer therefore means
approaching the least-squares memory d d times more slowly, at fixed
β \beta . The statement is not that KronLSTM forgets less for free, but
that its per-step write is d d times gentler in every direction
of an d 2 d^{2} -dimensional memory, where DeltaNet’s is concentrated on a
d d -dimensional one — the same trade-off that appears in the capacity
count ( d 2 d^{2} against d d exactly satisfiable associations) and in the
norm bound, seen through its time
constant. Long contexts are precisely the regime in which the number of
associations, not the speed of fitting any one of them, is binding.
Appendix A6 Further Results
A6.1 400M language models
Models with 400M/40B (Table A2 ) are closer to each other
in their performance.
KronLSTM has the best average and performs best
on HellaSwag, ARC-easy, ARC-challenge, and WinoGrande.
Mamba-3 is best on PIQA, KDA on LAMBADA, and
GDN-2 on WinoGrande where it ties with KronLSTM.
Compared with mLSTM, the matrix-memory xLSTM variant that KronLSTM builds
on, KronLSTM is better on five of the six tasks, raises the average by
2.1 points, and lowers LAMBADA perplexity from 43.40 to 28.28.
mLSTM is only marginally ahead on PIQA.

A6.2 Ablation on different formulations of KronLSTM
Table A3 ablates the components of the KronLSTM
write rule at 1.5B/100B. We report Avg-6, the mean accuracy over the five
commonsense reasoning tasks and LAMBADA, together with LAMBADA perplexity,
the average over the six-task real-world recall suite at 2k context, and
the average over the six RULER NIAH tasks of Table 3 at
4k, 8k and 16k. Three variants are compared with the base model of
( 10 ):
(1) the ℓ 2 \ell_{2} -normalization of 𝒗 t \bm{v}_{t} is removed;
(2) erase and write are coupled by using the target c t c_{t} instead of
c ~ t = c t / β t \tilde{c}_{t}=c_{t}/\beta_{t} , so that β t \beta_{t} scales both terms as in Gated
DeltaNet;
(3) the write gate is fixed to the unit target c t ≡ 1 c_{t}\equiv 1 .
On language modeling, variants (1) and (2) stay within 0.3 accuracy points
and 0.25 perplexity points of the base model, with the coupled variant
marginally ahead on both. Fixing the target costs 1.6 accuracy points and
0.8 perplexity points, the largest degradation among the ablations. On the
recall suite, variants (1) and (2) gain 1.0 and 0.5 points, respectively.
On NIAH the ordering reverses: at 4k the base model leads the coupled
variant by 12.6 points and the unnormalized variant by 19.9 points, at 8k
the unnormalized variant is ahead by 2.2 points, and at 16k the base model
leads all three variants. The learned write gate and the decoupled write
therefore matter most for retrieval within the pretraining context, while
their effect on language modeling is small.

Appendix A7 Pretraining Details
All models are pretrained from scratch using lm-engine Mishra (2024) with Triton kernels from flash-linear-attention Yang and Zhang (2026) whenever available. Every mixer in a comparison shares the corpus, the
tokenizer, the optimizer, the schedule, the batch size, and the seed; we only change the sequence mixer blocks.
The models were trained on 16 nodes of 4 × \times NVIDIA GH200. Training is pure data parallelism under the FSDP-2 with bf16 mixed-precision.
Data and tokenization
We train on the FineWeb-Edu 100BT sample, tokenized with the Llama 2 tokenizer with its standard vocab size of 32,000. Documents are concatenated, separated by the EOS token, and packed into fixed-length sequences of 8192 tokens; This is applied identically to every experiment.
Architecture
All models are decoder-only and identical outside the sequence mixer. We implemented the block structures of every baseline as in the original works. Mamba-3 was trained using the standard expansion factor of 2, a dstate of 128, and a head dimension of 64. For Mamba-3, we utilized the SISO formulation. For the other baselines and our method, we utilized d q ​ k = d v = 128 d_{qk}=d_{v}=128 . This makes the state size equal across all models.
1.5B models have 24 24 layers, a hidden dimension of 2048 2048 with 16 heads. The 400M models have 24 layers with a hidden dimension of 1024 1024 and 4 heads (here d v = 256 d_{v}=256 ).
Optimization
optimizer
AdamW, β = ( 0.9 , 0.95 ) \beta=(0.9,0.95) , ϵ = 10 − 10 \epsilon=10^{-10}
peak learning rate
3 × 10 − 4 3\times 10^{-4}
schedule
linear warmup over 10% of steps, then cosine to 0.1 × 0.1\times peak ( 3 × 10 − 5 3\times 10^{-5} )
weight decay
0.1, excluding norms, biases, A log A_{\log} , b Δ b_{\Delta} and the gate bias
gradient clipping
global norm 1.0
sequence length
8192
global batch
128 sequences = = 1,048,576 tokens per step
precision
bf16 mixed precision, fp32 optimiser state
A7.1 Long-context extension
The context-extended models of Section 3.3 branch
from the 1.5B pretraining runs at 75B tokens and are
trained for a further 25B tokens at a sequence length of 32,768,
so that both extended models finish at exactly 100B
tokens and are token-matched to the 8k-pretrained models. The architecture,
tokenizer, and all model hyperparameters are unchanged. The global batch is
held at the same 1M tokens per step, now as 32 sequences of 32,768
tokens instead of 128 sequences of 8,192.
Optimization.
The optimizer state is re-initialized at the
branch point for both models and the schedule restarts: linear warmup over
5% to a peak learning rate of 8.4 × 10 − 5 8.4\times 10^{-5} , which is
the value the parent cosine schedule had reached at the checkpoint, followed
by cosine decay over 23,800 steps to 0.1 × 0.1\times the peak. This mirrors
the warmup-and-decay shape used by Gao et al. (2024) . AdamW
hyperparameters, weight decay, and gradient clipping are as in
Appendix A7 .
Data.
The corpus takes the four long-document domains of
ProLong-64K ( Gao et al., 2024 ) , books, code, textbooks, and arXiv,
re-tokenized with the Llama 2 tokenizer, and blends them at the ratios of
Gao et al. (2024) with FineWeb-Edu in place of their short-context
mixture. Documents are concatenated and packed into sequences of 32,768
tokens as in pretraining.
Systems.
Training runs on 8 nodes of 4 × 4\times NVIDIA GH200
with ZeRO stage 2 and block-wise gradient checkpointing under bf16 mixed
precision.
A7.2 State Tracking Experiments
State-tracking tasks.
Each sample is a token sequence of length ∼ 𝒰 ⁡ [ 2,128 ] \sim\mathcal{U}[2,128]
padded to the context length, with a single supervised position at its end.
Parity : i.i.d. uniform bits, label the parity of the number of ones
(chance 50 % 50\% ). S 3 S_{3} : i.i.d. uniform transpositions
( 0 1 ) , ( 0 2 ) , ( 1 2 ) (0\,1),(0\,2),(1\,2) , label the prefix product x 1 ∘ ⋯ ∘ x ℓ x_{1}\circ\dots\circ x_{\ell}
as one of the six group elements (chance 16.7 % 16.7\% ; tracking parity alone gives
33.3 % 33.3\% ). Modular arithmetic : an expression
d 1 ​ o 1 ​ d 2 ​ … ​ d k = d_{1}\,o_{1}\,d_{2}\dots d_{k}\,= with uniform digits in { 0 , … , 4 } \{0,\dots,4\} and
operators in { + , − , × } \{+,-,\times\} , evaluated with operator precedence modulo 5,
the result supervised at the = token (chance 20 % 20\% ). Samples are
generated on the fly and deterministically from the run seed (seeds 1-3);
evaluation uses 50,000 50{,}000 fresh sequences per length L ∈ { 128,256,512 , 1024 } L\in\{128,256,512,1024\} .
Models and training.
All models share a 128-dimensional embedding, pre-norm residual blocks
(RMSNorm, no feed-forward sublayer) and a linear read-out;
they differ only in the mixer: Gated DeltaNet with the eigenvalue range
extended to [ − 1 , 1 ] [-1,1] ( Grazzi et al., 2025 ) , Mamba-3 (SISO, expansion 2,
state size 128, data-dependent RoPE), and KronLSTM.
For optimizer we used AdamW with weight decay 0.1 0.1 , batch size 128, bf16. Parity and
S 3 S_{3} : two blocks, constant learning rate ∈ { 10 − 3 , 10 − 4 } \in\{10^{-3},10^{-4}\} , 50k steps,
gradient clipping 100. Modular arithmetic requires depth and a longer budget
for all three architectures: three blocks, 150k steps. We report end-of-training accuracy, mean ± \pm standard
deviation over three seeds, at the learning rate with the best mean accuracy
over L ∈ { 256 } L\in\{256\} .
Appendix A8 Chunkwise-Parallel Training of KronLSTM
The KronLSTM update ( 10 ) revises the matrix memory by a
rank-one term whose coefficient depends on the memory only through a
scalar. It therefore admits the chunkwise-parallel training scheme of
DeltaNet ( Yang et al., 2024 ) , Gated DeltaNet ( Yang et al., 2025 ) , Kimi Delta
Attention ( Kimi Team et al., 2025 ) , and Gated DeltaNet-2
( Hatamizadeh et al., 2026 ) : the sequence is split into chunks of length
C C , all token interactions inside a chunk are expressed as dense matrix
products and one small triangular solve, and the memory is propagated only
between chunks. Three points differ from the delta rule.
(i) The triangular solve is built on the Gram matrix of the Kronecker
features 𝒂 t = 𝒌 t ⊗ 𝒗 t \bm{a}_{t}=\bm{k}_{t}\otimes\bm{v}_{t} , which by the mixed-product property
is the Hadamard product of a (decay-weighted) key Gram and the value Gram;
the d k ​ d v d_{k}d_{v} -dimensional features are never formed.
(ii) The pseudo-values are scalar multiples u r ​ 𝒗 r u_{r}\bm{v}_{r} of the values.
(iii) The incoming memory of a chunk enters the solve through C C scalar
scores 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝑺 0 ​ ( 𝜸 r ⊙ 𝒌 r ) \bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\bm{S}_{0}(\bm{\gamma}_{r}\odot\bm{k}_{r}) instead of the
d v d_{v} -dimensional read-outs 𝑺 0 ​ 𝒌 r \bm{S}_{0}\bm{k}_{r} of the delta rule. The vector
forget gate is handled as in KDA and Gated DeltaNet-2 by absorbing the
cumulative decay into an asymmetric pair of keys.
A8.1 Setting and notation
We consider one chunk of C C tokens and suppress the chunk index. Let
𝑺 0 ∈ ℝ d v × d k \bm{S}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}\times d_{k}} be the memory entering the chunk and let
𝒒 r , 𝒌 r ∈ ℝ d k \bm{q}_{r},\bm{k}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}} , 𝒗 r ∈ ℝ d v \bm{v}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}} , β r , c r ∈ ℝ \beta_{r},c_{r}\in\mathbb{R}
and 𝐟 r ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) d k \mathbf{f}_{r}\in(0,1)^{d_{k}} , 𝑭 r = diag ⁡ ( 𝐟 r ) \bm{F}_{r}=\operatorname{diag}(\mathbf{f}_{r}) , be the inputs and
gates of the r r -th token, r = 1 , … , C r=1,\dots,C . Writing 𝑺 r \bm{S}_{r} for the memory
after token r r , the update ( 10 ) and the read-out
are 1 1
1
This is ( 10 ) with the time index shifted by
one, so that 𝑺 r \bm{S}_{r} denotes the memory after the write of token r r . The
read-out uses this memory, 𝒉 r = 𝑺 r ​ 𝒒 r \bm{h}_{r}=\bm{S}_{r}\bm{q}_{r} , i.e., the query of token
r r sees the association written at token r r , as in DeltaNet and its gated
variants and in our kernels.
𝑺 r = 𝑺 r − 1 ​ 𝑭 r + u r ​ 𝒗 r ​ 𝒌 r ⊤ , u r := c r − β r ​ y r , y r := 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝑺 r − 1 ​ 𝑭 r ​ 𝒌 r , 𝒉 r = 𝑺 r ​ 𝒒 r . \bm{S}_{r}\;=\;\bm{S}_{r-1}\bm{F}_{r}\;+\;u_{r}\,\bm{v}_{r}\bm{k}_{r}^{\top},\qquad u_{r}\;:=\;c_{r}-\beta_{r}\,y_{r},\qquad y_{r}\;:=\;\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\,\bm{S}_{r-1}\bm{F}_{r}\,\bm{k}_{r},\qquad\bm{h}_{r}\;=\;\bm{S}_{r}\bm{q}_{r}.
(A149)
We call y r y_{r} the score of the pair ( 𝒌 r , 𝒗 r ) (\bm{k}_{r},\bm{v}_{r}) on the decayed memory
and u r u_{r} the write coefficient, the coefficient of the rank-one write along
𝒗 r ​ 𝒌 r ⊤ \bm{v}_{r}\bm{k}_{r}^{\top} . It is the only quantity in ( A149 ) that
depends on the memory.
Cumulative decay.
With ℓ r := ∑ i ≤ r log ⁡ 𝐟 i \bm{\ell}_{r}:=\sum_{i\leq r}\log\mathbf{f}_{i} (a cumulative sum inside
the chunk) we set
𝜸 r := exp ( ℓ r ) = 𝐟 1 ⊙ ⋯ ⊙ 𝐟 r ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] d k , 𝜸 0 := 1 , 𝚪 r := diag ( 𝜸 r ) = 𝑭 1 ⋯ 𝑭 r . \bm{\gamma}_{r}\;:=\;\exp(\bm{\ell}_{r})\;=\;\mathbf{f}_{1}\odot\cdots\odot\mathbf{f}_{r}\;\in\;(0,1]^{d_{k}},\qquad\bm{\gamma}_{0}\;:=\;\bm{1},\qquad\bm{\Gamma}_{r}\;:=\;\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\gamma}_{r})\;=\;\bm{F}_{1}\cdots\bm{F}_{r}.
(A150)
All products, quotients and exponentials of 𝜸 \bm{\gamma} ’s are element-wise over
the key dimension. For s ≤ r s\leq r the ratio 𝜸 r / 𝜸 s = 𝐟 s + 1 ⊙ ⋯ ⊙ 𝐟 r \bm{\gamma}_{r}/\bm{\gamma}_{s}=\mathbf{f}_{s+1}\odot\cdots\odot\mathbf{f}_{r} lies in ( 0 , 1 ] d k (0,1]^{d_{k}} ; it is the decay that a key
written at token s s has undergone by token r r . Four decay-scaled copies of
the keys and queries appear:
𝒌 ^ r := 𝜸 r ⊙ 𝒌 r , 𝒌 ¯ r := 𝒌 r / 𝜸 r , 𝒌 ˘ r := ( 𝜸 C / 𝜸 r ) ⊙ 𝒌 r , 𝒒 ^ r := 𝜸 r ⊙ 𝒒 r . \widehat{\bm{k}}_{r}\;:=\;\bm{\gamma}_{r}\odot\bm{k}_{r},\qquad\bar{\bm{k}}_{r}\;:=\;\bm{k}_{r}/\bm{\gamma}_{r},\qquad\breve{\bm{k}}_{r}\;:=\;(\bm{\gamma}_{C}/\bm{\gamma}_{r})\odot\bm{k}_{r},\qquad\widehat{\bm{q}}_{r}\;:=\;\bm{\gamma}_{r}\odot\bm{q}_{r}.
(A151)
𝒌 ^ r \widehat{\bm{k}}_{r} is the key with which the memory is scored,
𝒌 ¯ r \bar{\bm{k}}_{r} the key along which it is written in the decay-normalized frame
of Section A8.2 , and 𝒌 ˘ r \breve{\bm{k}}_{r} the key as it
appears in the memory at the end of the chunk.
Stacked matrices.
𝑲 , 𝑸 ∈ ℝ C × d k \bm{K},\bm{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{C\times d_{k}} and 𝑽 ∈ ℝ C × d v \bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{C\times d_{v}} stack
𝒌 r ⊤ \bm{k}_{r}^{\top} , 𝒒 r ⊤ \bm{q}_{r}^{\top} , 𝒗 r ⊤ \bm{v}_{r}^{\top} as rows; 𝑲 ^ , 𝑲 ¯ , 𝑲 ˘ , 𝑸 ^ \widehat{\bm{K}},\bar{\bm{K}},\breve{\bm{K}},\widehat{\bm{Q}} stack the vectors of
( A151 ); 𝜷 , 𝒄 , 𝒖 , 𝒑 ∈ ℝ C \bm{\beta},\bm{c},\bm{u},\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{C} stack the scalars;
𝑯 ∈ ℝ C × d v \bm{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{C\times d_{v}} stacks the read-outs 𝒉 r ⊤ \bm{h}_{r}^{\top} . The three
C × C C\times C matrices
𝑮 r ​ s k ​ k := 1 [ r > s ] 𝒌 ^ r ⊤ 𝒌 ¯ s = 1 [ r > s ] ∑ d = 1 d k k r ​ d k s ​ d γ r ​ d γ s ​ d , 𝑮 v ​ v := 𝑽 𝑽 ⊤ , 𝑮 r ​ s q ​ k := 1 [ r ≥ s ] 𝒒 ^ r ⊤ 𝒌 ¯ s \bm{G}^{kk}_{rs}\;:=\;\mathbbm{1}[r>s]\;\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r}^{\top}\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}\;=\;\mathbbm{1}[r>s]\sum_{d=1}^{d_{k}}k_{rd}\,k_{sd}\,\frac{\gamma_{rd}}{\gamma_{sd}},\qquad\bm{G}^{vv}\;:=\;\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top},\qquad\bm{G}^{qk}_{rs}\;:=\;\mathbbm{1}[r\geq s]\;\widehat{\bm{q}}_{r}^{\top}\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}
(A152)
are the decay-weighted key Gram (strictly lower triangular), the value
Gram, and the decay-weighted read-out Gram (lower triangular with
diagonal). All entries of 𝑮 k ​ k \bm{G}^{kk} and 𝑮 q ​ k \bm{G}^{qk} involve only ratios
𝜸 r / 𝜸 s \bm{\gamma}_{r}/\bm{\gamma}_{s} with s ≤ r s\leq r , hence only factors in ( 0 , 1 ] (0,1] .
A8.2 Decay-normalized recurrence
The forget gate acts on the key dimension by a diagonal matrix, and
diagonal matrices commute. This allows the cumulative decay to be taken out
of the recurrence.
Proposition A4 (Decay-normalized recurrence) .
Let 𝐒 ^ r := 𝐒 r ​ 𝚪 r − 1 \widehat{\bm{S}}_{r}:=\bm{S}_{r}\bm{\Gamma}_{r}^{-1} , so that 𝐒 ^ 0 = 𝐒 0 \widehat{\bm{S}}_{0}=\bm{S}_{0} . Then, for r = 1 , … , C r=1,\dots,C ,
𝑺 ^ r = 𝑺 ^ r − 1 + u r ​ 𝒗 r ​ 𝒌 ¯ r ⊤ , y r = 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝑺 ^ r − 1 ​ 𝒌 ^ r , \widehat{\bm{S}}_{r}\;=\;\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r-1}\;+\;u_{r}\,\bm{v}_{r}\bar{\bm{k}}_{r}^{\top},\qquad y_{r}\;=\;\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\,\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r-1}\,\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r},
(A153)
and consequently
𝑺 ^ r = 𝑺 0 + ∑ s ≤ r u s ​ 𝒗 s ​ 𝒌 ¯ s ⊤ . \widehat{\bm{S}}_{r}\;=\;\bm{S}_{0}\;+\;\sum_{s\leq r}u_{s}\,\bm{v}_{s}\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}.
(A154)
Proof.
Since 𝚪 r = 𝚪 r − 1 ​ 𝑭 r \bm{\Gamma}_{r}=\bm{\Gamma}_{r-1}\bm{F}_{r} , we have 𝑺 r − 1 ​ 𝑭 r = 𝑺 ^ r − 1 ​ 𝚪 r − 1 ​ 𝑭 r = 𝑺 ^ r − 1 ​ 𝚪 r \bm{S}_{r-1}\bm{F}_{r}=\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r-1}\bm{\Gamma}_{r-1}\bm{F}_{r}=\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r-1}\bm{\Gamma}_{r} .
Inserting this into ( A149 ) gives 𝑺 r = 𝑺 ^ r − 1 ​ 𝚪 r + u r ​ 𝒗 r ​ 𝒌 r ⊤ \bm{S}_{r}=\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r-1}\bm{\Gamma}_{r}+u_{r}\bm{v}_{r}\bm{k}_{r}^{\top} ; right-multiplication by 𝚪 r − 1 \bm{\Gamma}_{r}^{-1}
yields the first identity, because 𝒌 r ⊤ ​ 𝚪 r − 1 = 𝒌 ¯ r ⊤ \bm{k}_{r}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{r}^{-1}=\bar{\bm{k}}_{r}^{\top} . The second identity is y r = 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ ( 𝑺 ^ r − 1 ​ 𝚪 r ) ​ 𝒌 r = 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝑺 ^ r − 1 ​ ( 𝚪 r ​ 𝒌 r ) y_{r}=\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r-1}\bm{\Gamma}_{r})\bm{k}_{r}=\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r-1}(\bm{\Gamma}_{r}\bm{k}_{r}) . Unrolling the first identity from 𝑺 ^ 0 = 𝑺 0 \widehat{\bm{S}}_{0}=\bm{S}_{0}
gives ( A154 ).
∎
In the normalized frame the recurrence is the Kronecker Delta Rule without
forget gate, applied to an asymmetric key pair: the association is scored
with the decayed key 𝒌 ^ r \widehat{\bm{k}}_{r} and written along the un-decayed key
𝒌 ¯ r \bar{\bm{k}}_{r} . This is the same device by which Gated DeltaNet-2 absorbs
its channel-wise decay into asymmetric erase factors
( Hatamizadeh et al., 2026 ) . Equation ( A154 ) is the
additive representation of the memory, 𝑺 r = ( 𝑺 0 + ∑ s ≤ r u s ​ 𝒗 s ​ 𝒌 ¯ s ⊤ ) ​ 𝚪 r \bm{S}_{r}=\big(\bm{S}_{0}+\sum_{s\leq r}u_{s}\bm{v}_{s}\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}\big)\bm{\Gamma}_{r} ; the memory revisions do not have
to be materialized, only the C C scalars u s u_{s} .
A8.3 The write coefficients solve a unit lower triangular system
Proposition A5 (Write coefficients) .
Let p r := 𝐯 r ⊤ ​ 𝐒 0 ​ 𝐤 ^ r p_{r}:=\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\bm{S}_{0}\,\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r} be the score of the r r -th
pair on the incoming memory, and let
𝑵 := diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) ​ ( 𝑮 k ​ k ⊙ 𝑮 v ​ v ) ∈ ℝ C × C , 𝚲 := ( 𝑰 + 𝑵 ) − 1 . \bm{N}\;:=\;\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta})\,\big(\bm{G}^{kk}\odot\bm{G}^{vv}\big)\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{C\times C},\qquad\bm{\Lambda}\;:=\;(\bm{I}+\bm{N})^{-1}.
(A155)
𝑵 \bm{N} is strictly lower triangular, so 𝐈 + 𝐍 \bm{I}+\bm{N} is unit lower
triangular and invertible for every 𝛃 \bm{\beta} and every input. The write
coefficients are
𝒖 = 𝚲 ( 𝒄 − 𝜷 ⊙ 𝒑 ) = 𝚲 ​ 𝒄 ⏟ = : 𝒖 ∘ − 𝚲 diag ( 𝜷 ) 𝒑 . \bm{u}\;=\;\bm{\Lambda}\,\big(\bm{c}-\bm{\beta}\odot\bm{p}\big)\;=\;\underbrace{\bm{\Lambda}\,\bm{c}}_{=:\,\bm{u}^{\circ}}\;-\;\bm{\Lambda}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta})\,\bm{p}.
(A156)
Proof.
Insert ( A154 ) at r − 1 r-1 into the score in
( A153 ):
y r = 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝑺 0 ​ 𝒌 ^ r + ∑ s < r u s ​ ( 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝒗 s ) ​ ( 𝒌 ¯ s ⊤ ​ 𝒌 ^ r ) = p r + ∑ s < r ( 𝑮 k ​ k ⊙ 𝑮 v ​ v ) r ​ s ​ u s . y_{r}\;=\;\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\bm{S}_{0}\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r}\;+\;\sum_{s<r}u_{s}\,(\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\bm{v}_{s})(\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r})\;=\;p_{r}\;+\;\sum_{s<r}\big(\bm{G}^{kk}\odot\bm{G}^{vv}\big)_{rs}\,u_{s}.
Hence u r = c r − β r ​ y r u_{r}=c_{r}-\beta_{r}y_{r} reads u r + ∑ s < r 𝑵 r ​ s ​ u s = c r − β r ​ p r u_{r}+\sum_{s<r}\bm{N}_{rs}u_{s}=c_{r}-\beta_{r}p_{r} , which is row r r of ( 𝑰 + 𝑵 ) ​ 𝒖 = 𝒄 − 𝜷 ⊙ 𝒑 (\bm{I}+\bm{N})\bm{u}=\bm{c}-\bm{\beta}\odot\bm{p} .
∎
The Gram of the Kronecker features.
With 𝒂 ^ r := 𝒌 ^ r ⊗ 𝒗 r \widehat{\bm{a}}_{r}:=\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r}\otimes\bm{v}_{r} and 𝒂 ¯ s := 𝒌 ¯ s ⊗ 𝒗 s \bar{\bm{a}}_{s}:=\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s} , the mixed-product property of the Kronecker
product gives 𝒂 ^ r ⊤ ​ 𝒂 ¯ s = ( 𝒌 ^ r ⊤ ​ 𝒌 ¯ s ) ​ ( 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝒗 s ) \widehat{\bm{a}}_{r}^{\top}\bar{\bm{a}}_{s}=(\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r}^{\top}\bar{\bm{k}}_{s})(\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\bm{v}_{s}) , so 𝑮 k ​ k ⊙ 𝑮 v ​ v \bm{G}^{kk}\odot\bm{G}^{vv} is the
strictly lower triangular part of the Gram matrix of the decay-weighted
Kronecker features. Without forget gate ( 𝐟 r ≡ 𝟏 \mathbf{f}_{r}\equiv\bm{1} ) it is the
strictly lower part of the Gram matrix ( 𝒂 r ⊤ ​ 𝒂 s ) r ​ s (\bm{a}_{r}^{\top}\bm{a}_{s})_{rs} of the
features 𝒂 r = 𝒌 r ⊗ 𝒗 r \bm{a}_{r}=\bm{k}_{r}\otimes\bm{v}_{r} , i.e., exactly the matrix that the
UT transform of DeltaNet ( Yang et al., 2024 ) builds from 𝑲 ​ 𝑲 ⊤ \bm{K}\bm{K}^{\top} , with
the feature 𝒂 r \bm{a}_{r} in place of 𝒌 r \bm{k}_{r} as anticipated by the vectorized
form ( 4 ). The d k ​ d v d_{k}d_{v} -dimensional features are never
formed: two C × C C\times C Gram matrices of d k d_{k} - and d v d_{v} -dimensional
vectors and one Hadamard product suffice.
Decoupled erase and write.
The write gate c r c_{r} enters only the right-hand side of the solve, the
erase gate β r \beta_{r} enters the system matrix and the coupling to 𝑺 0 \bm{S}_{0} .
The state-independent part 𝒖 ∘ = 𝚲 ​ 𝒄 \bm{u}^{\circ}=\bm{\Lambda}\bm{c} and the matrix 𝚲 ​ diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) \bm{\Lambda}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta}) can be computed for all chunks in parallel; they are the
analogues of the pseudo-values 𝑼 = 𝑻 ​ 𝑽 \bm{U}=\bm{T}\bm{V} and of 𝑾 = 𝑻 ​ 𝑲 \bm{W}=\bm{T}\bm{K} in
DeltaNet. The only sequential quantity is the vector of scores 𝒑 \bm{p} .
A8.4 Chunkwise form
Theorem A11 (Chunkwise KronLSTM) .
Let 𝐕 ~ := diag ⁡ ( 𝐮 ) ​ 𝐕 ∈ ℝ C × d v \widetilde{\bm{V}}:=\operatorname{diag}(\bm{u})\,\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{C\times d_{v}} be the
matrix of pseudo-values, with rows u r ​ 𝐯 r ⊤ u_{r}\bm{v}_{r}^{\top} , where 𝐮 \bm{u} is given by
Proposition A5 and
𝒑 = ( ( 𝑽 ​ 𝑺 0 ) ⊙ 𝑲 ^ ) ​ 1 (row sums of a C × d k matrix). \bm{p}\;=\;\big((\bm{V}\bm{S}_{0})\odot\widehat{\bm{K}}\big)\,\bm{1}\qquad\text{(row sums of a $C\times d_{k}$ matrix).}
(A157)
Then the memory at the end of the chunk and the read-outs of the chunk are
𝑺 C \displaystyle\bm{S}_{C}
= 𝑺 0 ​ 𝚪 C + 𝑽 ~ ⊤ ​ 𝑲 ˘ ∈ ℝ d v × d k , \displaystyle\;=\;\bm{S}_{0}\,\bm{\Gamma}_{C}\;+\;\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\top}\breve{\bm{K}}\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}\times d_{k}},
(A158)
𝑯 \displaystyle\bm{H}
= 𝑸 ^ ​ 𝑺 0 ⊤ + 𝑮 q ​ k ​ 𝑽 ~ ∈ ℝ C × d v . \displaystyle\;=\;\widehat{\bm{Q}}\,\bm{S}_{0}^{\top}\;+\;\bm{G}^{qk}\,\widetilde{\bm{V}}\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{C\times d_{v}}.
(A159)
Proof.
Equation ( A157 ) is p r = 𝒗 r ⊤ ​ 𝑺 0 ​ 𝒌 ^ r = ∑ d ( 𝑽 ​ 𝑺 0 ) r ​ d ​ K ^ r ​ d p_{r}=\bm{v}_{r}^{\top}\bm{S}_{0}\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r}=\sum_{d}(\bm{V}\bm{S}_{0})_{rd}\,\widehat{K}_{rd} . For the state, 𝑺 C = 𝑺 ^ C ​ 𝚪 C = 𝑺 0 ​ 𝚪 C + ∑ s ≤ C u s ​ 𝒗 s ​ 𝒌 ¯ s ⊤ ​ 𝚪 C \bm{S}_{C}=\widehat{\bm{S}}_{C}\bm{\Gamma}_{C}=\bm{S}_{0}\bm{\Gamma}_{C}+\sum_{s\leq C}u_{s}\bm{v}_{s}\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{C} by ( A154 ), and 𝒌 ¯ s ⊤ ​ 𝚪 C = ( ( 𝜸 C / 𝜸 s ) ⊙ 𝒌 s ) ⊤ = 𝒌 ˘ s ⊤ \bar{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{C}=\big((\bm{\gamma}_{C}/\bm{\gamma}_{s})\odot\bm{k}_{s}\big)^{\top}=\breve{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top} ,
which is ( A158 ). For the read-out, 𝒉 r = 𝑺 r ​ 𝒒 r = 𝑺 ^ r ​ 𝚪 r ​ 𝒒 r = 𝑺 ^ r ​ 𝒒 ^ r = 𝑺 0 ​ 𝒒 ^ r + ∑ s ≤ r u s ​ ( 𝒌 ¯ s ⊤ ​ 𝒒 ^ r ) ​ 𝒗 s \bm{h}_{r}=\bm{S}_{r}\bm{q}_{r}=\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r}\bm{\Gamma}_{r}\bm{q}_{r}=\widehat{\bm{S}}_{r}\widehat{\bm{q}}_{r}=\bm{S}_{0}\widehat{\bm{q}}_{r}+\sum_{s\leq r}u_{s}\,(\bar{\bm{k}}_{s}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{q}}_{r})\,\bm{v}_{s} by ( A154 ); transposing and
stacking over r r gives ( A159 ), the coefficient of u s ​ 𝒗 s ⊤ u_{s}\bm{v}_{s}^{\top} in row r r being 𝑮 r ​ s q ​ k \bm{G}^{qk}_{rs} .
∎
Indexing the chunks by [ n ] [n] and writing 𝑺 [ n ] \bm{S}_{[n]} for the memory entering
chunk n n , ( A158 ) is the chunk-level recurrence
𝑺 [ n + 1 ] = 𝑺 [ n ] ​ 𝚪 [ n ] , C + 𝑽 ~ [ n ] ⊤ ​ 𝑲 ˘ [ n ] , \bm{S}_{[n+1]}\;=\;\bm{S}_{[n]}\,\bm{\Gamma}_{[n],C}\;+\;\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{[n]}^{\top}\breve{\bm{K}}_{[n]},
(A160)
with 𝑺 [ 0 ] = 𝟎 \bm{S}_{[0]}=\bm{0} (or a given initial memory). Everything in
( A152 )–( A159 ) except 𝒑 \bm{p} , 𝒖 \bm{u} and
𝑽 ~ \widetilde{\bm{V}} is local to a chunk and is computed for all chunks at
once; the dependence 𝑺 [ n ] → 𝒑 → 𝒖 → 𝑺 [ n + 1 ] \bm{S}_{[n]}\to\bm{p}\to\bm{u}\to\bm{S}_{[n+1]} is the only
sequential part. Compared with the delta-rule family, the pseudo-value of
token r r is the value 𝒗 r \bm{v}_{r} scaled by the scalar u r u_{r} , and the memory
couples into the chunk through the C C scalars 𝒑 \bm{p} rather than through
the C C vectors 𝑺 0 ​ 𝒌 r ∈ ℝ d v \bm{S}_{0}\bm{k}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}} : computing 𝒑 \bm{p} needs one
matrix product 𝑽 ​ 𝑺 0 \bm{V}\bm{S}_{0} and a row-wise contraction over the value
dimension, which is where a kernel that tiles the memory over d v d_{v} must
exchange partial sums.
A8.5 WY representation of the chunk transition
In vectorized form, 𝒔 = vec ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) \bm{s}=\vecop(\bm{S}) with vec ⁡ ( 𝒗 ​ 𝒌 ⊤ ) = 𝒌 ⊗ 𝒗 \vecop(\bm{v}\bm{k}^{\top})=\bm{k}\otimes\bm{v} and vec ⁡ ( 𝑺 ​ 𝑭 ) = ( 𝑭 ⊗ 𝑰 d v ) ​ vec ⁡ ( 𝑺 ) \vecop(\bm{S}\bm{F})=(\bm{F}\otimes\bm{I}_{d_{v}})\vecop(\bm{S}) ,
one step of ( A149 ) is 𝒔 r = ( 𝑰 − β r ​ 𝒂 r ​ 𝒂 r ⊤ ) ​ ( 𝑭 r ⊗ 𝑰 d v ) ​ 𝒔 r − 1 + c r ​ 𝒂 r \bm{s}_{r}=(\bm{I}-\beta_{r}\bm{a}_{r}\bm{a}_{r}^{\top})(\bm{F}_{r}\otimes\bm{I}_{d_{v}})\,\bm{s}_{r-1}+c_{r}\bm{a}_{r} with 𝒂 r = 𝒌 r ⊗ 𝒗 r \bm{a}_{r}=\bm{k}_{r}\otimes\bm{v}_{r} : a generalized Householder transition, as in
( 4 ), preceded by a diagonal decay.
Theorem A11 is the WY representation ( Bischof and VanLoan, 1987 )
of the product of these factors.
Corollary A7 (WY form) .
Let 𝐀 ^ := [ 𝐤 ^ 1 ⊗ 𝐯 1 , … , 𝐤 ^ C ⊗ 𝐯 C ] \widehat{\bm{A}}:=[\widehat{\bm{k}}_{1}\otimes\bm{v}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\bm{k}}_{C}\otimes\bm{v}_{C}] and 𝐀 ˘ := [ 𝐤 ˘ 1 ⊗ 𝐯 1 , … , 𝐤 ˘ C ⊗ 𝐯 C ] \breve{\bm{A}}:=[\breve{\bm{k}}_{1}\otimes\bm{v}_{1},\dots,\breve{\bm{k}}_{C}\otimes\bm{v}_{C}] in ℝ d k ​ d v × C \mathbb{R}^{d_{k}d_{v}\times C} .
Then 𝐬 C = 𝐏 ​ 𝐬 0 + 𝐝 \bm{s}_{C}=\bm{P}\,\bm{s}_{0}+\bm{d} with
𝑷 = ∏ r = C 1 ( 𝑰 − β r ​ 𝒂 r ​ 𝒂 r ⊤ ) ​ ( 𝑭 r ⊗ 𝑰 d v ) = ( 𝚪 C ⊗ 𝑰 d v ) − 𝑨 ˘ ​ 𝚲 ​ diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) ​ 𝑨 ^ ⊤ , 𝒅 = 𝑨 ˘ ​ 𝚲 ​ 𝒄 . \bm{P}\;=\;\prod_{r=C}^{1}(\bm{I}-\beta_{r}\bm{a}_{r}\bm{a}_{r}^{\top})(\bm{F}_{r}\otimes\bm{I}_{d_{v}})\;=\;(\bm{\Gamma}_{C}\otimes\bm{I}_{d_{v}})\;-\;\breve{\bm{A}}\,\bm{\Lambda}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta})\,\widehat{\bm{A}}^{\top},\qquad\bm{d}\;=\;\breve{\bm{A}}\,\bm{\Lambda}\,\bm{c}.
(A161)
Proof.
By ( A158 ), 𝒔 C = ( 𝚪 C ⊗ 𝑰 ) ​ 𝒔 0 + ∑ r u r ​ ( 𝒌 ˘ r ⊗ 𝒗 r ) = ( 𝚪 C ⊗ 𝑰 ) ​ 𝒔 0 + 𝑨 ˘ ​ 𝒖 \bm{s}_{C}=(\bm{\Gamma}_{C}\otimes\bm{I})\bm{s}_{0}+\sum_{r}u_{r}(\breve{\bm{k}}_{r}\otimes\bm{v}_{r})=(\bm{\Gamma}_{C}\otimes\bm{I})\bm{s}_{0}+\breve{\bm{A}}\bm{u} . Since p r = ⟨ 𝒗 r ​ 𝒌 ^ r ⊤ , 𝑺 0 ⟩ F = 𝒂 ^ r ⊤ ​ 𝒔 0 p_{r}=\langle\bm{v}_{r}\widehat{\bm{k}}_{r}^{\top},\bm{S}_{0}\rangle_{F}=\widehat{\bm{a}}_{r}^{\top}\bm{s}_{0} , we have 𝒑 = 𝑨 ^ ⊤ ​ 𝒔 0 \bm{p}=\widehat{\bm{A}}^{\top}\bm{s}_{0} , and
( A156 ) gives 𝒖 = 𝚲 ​ 𝒄 − 𝚲 ​ diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) ​ 𝑨 ^ ⊤ ​ 𝒔 0 \bm{u}=\bm{\Lambda}\bm{c}-\bm{\Lambda}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta})\widehat{\bm{A}}^{\top}\bm{s}_{0} .
∎
The correction to the diagonal decay has rank at most C C , as for DeltaNet,
where 𝑷 = 𝑰 − ∑ s 𝒘 s ​ 𝒌 s ⊤ \bm{P}=\bm{I}-\sum_{s}\bm{w}_{s}\bm{k}_{s}^{\top} . The difference is that the
columns of 𝑨 ˘ ​ 𝚲 ​ diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) \breve{\bm{A}}\bm{\Lambda}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta}) are linear combinations of
Kronecker products 𝒌 ˘ s ⊗ 𝒗 s \breve{\bm{k}}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s} and are in general not
themselves Kronecker products, so the correction is not of the delta-rule
form ∑ s ( 𝑺 0 ​ 𝒘 s ) ​ 𝒌 s ⊤ \sum_{s}(\bm{S}_{0}\bm{w}_{s})\bm{k}_{s}^{\top} , in which the memory enters through
the d v d_{v} -dimensional read-outs 𝑺 0 ​ 𝒘 s \bm{S}_{0}\bm{w}_{s} . Instead the memory enters
through the C C scalars 𝒑 \bm{p} , and each pseudo-value is 𝒗 s \bm{v}_{s} scaled by a
linear functional of 𝑺 0 \bm{S}_{0} . Table A5 summarizes
the roles of the chunk quantities in the delta-rule family and in KronLSTM.

A8.6 Variants
The derivation uses only the structure “diagonal decay on the key side,
rank-one write along 𝒗 r ​ 𝒌 r ⊤ \bm{v}_{r}\bm{k}_{r}^{\top} with a coefficient affine in the
scalar score”:
•
Coupled erase and write (variant “ β \beta on both sides” of
Table A3 ; target c r c_{r} instead of c ~ r = c r / β r \tilde{c}_{r}=c_{r}/\beta_{r} ): replace 𝒄 \bm{c} by 𝜷 ⊙ 𝒄 \bm{\beta}\odot\bm{c} in ( A156 ).
Unit target (variant “non-learnable target”): 𝒄 = 𝟏 \bm{c}=\bm{1} , so
𝒖 ∘ = 𝚲 ​ 𝟏 \bm{u}^{\circ}=\bm{\Lambda}\bm{1} .
•
State tracking , β r ∈ ( 0 , 2 ) \beta_{r}\in(0,2)
(Section 3.4 ): nothing changes. 𝑰 + 𝑵 \bm{I}+\bm{N} is unit
lower triangular for every 𝜷 \bm{\beta} , so the solve needs no condition on the
gates or on the data.
•
Scalar forget gate 𝐟 r = f r ​ 𝟏 \mathbf{f}_{r}=f_{r}\bm{1} (as in Gated DeltaNet):
𝜸 r \bm{\gamma}_{r} is a scalar and 𝑮 r ​ s k ​ k = 𝟙 [ r > s ] ( 𝒌 r ⊤ 𝒌 s ) γ r / γ s \bm{G}^{kk}_{rs}=\mathbbm{1}[{r>s}]\,(\bm{k}_{r}^{\top}\bm{k}_{s})\,\gamma_{r}/\gamma_{s} . No forget gate :
𝑮 k ​ k = stril ⁡ ( 𝑲 ​ 𝑲 ⊤ ) \bm{G}^{kk}=\operatorname{stril}(\bm{K}\bm{K}^{\top}) and 𝑲 ˘ = 𝑲 ^ = 𝑲 ¯ = 𝑲 \breve{\bm{K}}=\widehat{\bm{K}}=\bar{\bm{K}}=\bm{K} .
•
Values of arbitrary norm. The kernels accept unnormalized
values by the substitution β r ← β r / ‖ 𝒗 r ‖ 2 \beta_{r}\leftarrow\beta_{r}/\left\lVert\bm{v}_{r}\right\rVert^{2}
(the erase step is capped so that β r ​ ‖ 𝒂 r ‖ 2 = β r \beta_{r}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{r}\right\rVert^{2}=\beta_{r} for
unit-norm keys and the contraction condition of
Property A6 holds independently of
‖ 𝒗 r ‖ \left\lVert\bm{v}_{r}\right\rVert ); the write gate is unaffected. With the
ℓ 2 \ell_{2} -normalized values of Section 2.2 this is
( A149 ).
A8.7 Algorithm, cost and implementation
Algorithm (forward pass, one head).
Given 𝑸 , 𝑲 ∈ ℝ L × d k \bm{Q},\bm{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d_{k}} , 𝑽 ∈ ℝ L × d v \bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d_{v}} ,
𝜷 , 𝒄 ∈ ℝ L \bm{\beta},\bm{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{L} , log ⁡ 𝐟 ∈ ℝ L × d k \log\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d_{k}} , a chunk length
C C and an initial memory 𝑺 [ 0 ] \bm{S}_{[0]} :
1.
Chunk-local, in parallel over chunks. For every chunk:
ℓ ← cumsum ⁡ ( log ⁡ 𝐟 ) \bm{\ell}\leftarrow\operatorname{cumsum}(\log\mathbf{f}) within the chunk,
𝜸 ← exp ⁡ ( ℓ ) \bm{\gamma}\leftarrow\exp(\bm{\ell}) , and 𝑲 ^ , 𝑲 ¯ , 𝑲 ˘ , 𝑸 ^ \widehat{\bm{K}},\bar{\bm{K}},\breve{\bm{K}},\widehat{\bm{Q}} as in ( A151 );
𝑮 k ​ k , 𝑮 v ​ v , 𝑮 q ​ k \bm{G}^{kk},\bm{G}^{vv},\bm{G}^{qk} as in ( A152 ), the entries of
𝑮 k ​ k \bm{G}^{kk} and 𝑮 q ​ k \bm{G}^{qk} via exp ⁡ ( ℓ r − ℓ s ) \exp(\bm{\ell}_{r}-\bm{\ell}_{s}) for s ≤ r s\leq r ;
𝑵 ← diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) ​ ( 𝑮 k ​ k ⊙ 𝑮 v ​ v ) \bm{N}\leftarrow\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta})(\bm{G}^{kk}\odot\bm{G}^{vv}) and
𝚲 ← ( 𝑰 + 𝑵 ) − 1 \bm{\Lambda}\leftarrow(\bm{I}+\bm{N})^{-1} by forward substitution;
𝒖 ∘ ← 𝚲 ​ 𝒄 \bm{u}^{\circ}\leftarrow\bm{\Lambda}\bm{c} and 𝚲 β ← 𝚲 ​ diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) \bm{\Lambda}_{\beta}\leftarrow\bm{\Lambda}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta}) .
2.
Scan, sequential over chunks. 𝑺 ← 𝑺 [ 0 ] \bm{S}\leftarrow\bm{S}_{[0]} ; for
n = 0 , 1 , … n=0,1,\dots :
𝒑 ← ( ( 𝑽 ​ 𝑺 ) ⊙ 𝑲 ^ ) ​ 𝟏 \bm{p}\leftarrow\big((\bm{V}\bm{S})\odot\widehat{\bm{K}}\big)\bm{1} ;
𝒖 ← 𝒖 ∘ − 𝚲 β ​ 𝒑 \bm{u}\leftarrow\bm{u}^{\circ}-\bm{\Lambda}_{\beta}\,\bm{p} ;
𝑽 ~ ← diag ⁡ ( 𝒖 ) ​ 𝑽 \widetilde{\bm{V}}\leftarrow\operatorname{diag}(\bm{u})\bm{V} ;
𝑯 [ n ] ← 𝑸 ^ ​ 𝑺 ⊤ + 𝑮 q ​ k ​ 𝑽 ~ \bm{H}_{[n]}\leftarrow\widehat{\bm{Q}}\bm{S}^{\top}+\bm{G}^{qk}\widetilde{\bm{V}} ;
𝑺 ← 𝑺 ​ diag ⁡ ( 𝜸 C ) + 𝑽 ~ ⊤ ​ 𝑲 ˘ \bm{S}\leftarrow\bm{S}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\gamma}_{C})+\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\top}\breve{\bm{K}} .
3.
Return 𝑯 ∈ ℝ L × d v \bm{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times d_{v}} and the final memory 𝑺 \bm{S} .
Cost.
Per chunk and head the algorithm performs the two C × C C\times C key Grams
( 2 ​ C 2 ​ d k 2C^{2}d_{k} multiply–adds), the value Gram ( C 2 ​ d v C^{2}d_{v} ), one unit lower
triangular solve applied to two right-hand sides ( O ⁡ ( C 3 ) O(C^{3}) , in practice on
16 × 16 16\times 16 blocks as in the delta-rule kernels), three products with the
memory, 𝑽 ​ 𝑺 \bm{V}\bm{S} , 𝑸 ^ ​ 𝑺 ⊤ \widehat{\bm{Q}}\bm{S}^{\top} and 𝑽 ~ ⊤ ​ 𝑲 ˘ \widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\top}\breve{\bm{K}} ( 3 ​ C ​ d k ​ d v 3Cd_{k}d_{v} ), and the intra-chunk product 𝑮 q ​ k ​ 𝑽 ~ \bm{G}^{qk}\widetilde{\bm{V}} ( C 2 ​ d v C^{2}d_{v} ). Over a sequence of length L L this is
O ⁡ ( L ​ C ​ ( d k + d v ) + L ​ d k ​ d v ) O\big(LC(d_{k}+d_{v})+Ld_{k}d_{v}\big) multiply–adds and O ⁡ ( L ​ C 2 ) O(LC^{2}) for
the solves, the same order as Gated DeltaNet and KDA; relative to KDA the
additional cost is the value Gram 𝑮 v ​ v \bm{G}^{vv} , and the coupling 𝒑 \bm{p}
replaces the product 𝑾 ​ 𝑺 0 \bm{W}\bm{S}_{0} of the delta rule at equal cost. The
memory is materialized once per chunk, not per token.
Implementation notes.
(i) The cumulative decays are kept in log space; the Gram entries use
exp ⁡ ( ℓ r − ℓ s ) \exp(\bm{\ell}_{r}-\bm{\ell}_{s}) for s ≤ r s\leq r only, so all decay factors
lie in ( 0 , 1 ] (0,1] and no quotient 1 / 𝜸 1/\bm{\gamma} is ever formed (the kernels use
base-2 logarithms and exp 2 \exp_{2} ). (ii) The triangular solve is the
precision-sensitive step and is carried out in IEEE fp32; the remaining
products run on tensor cores. (iii) In the fused kernel the solve is not
materialized as 𝒖 ∘ \bm{u}^{\circ} and 𝚲 β \bm{\Lambda}_{\beta} but applied inside the
scan as 𝒖 = 𝚲 ⁡ ( 𝒄 − 𝜷 ⊙ 𝒑 ) \bm{u}=\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{c}-\bm{\beta}\odot\bm{p}) . (iv) Because 𝒑 \bm{p} contracts
over the full value dimension, programs that tile the memory over d v d_{v}
exchange their partial row sums of ( 𝑽 ​ 𝑺 ) ⊙ 𝑲 ^ (\bm{V}\bm{S})\odot\widehat{\bm{K}} once per
chunk; this is a synchronization, not a bandwidth cost ( C C floats per
program against a d k × d v d_{k}\times d_{v} memory). (v) Padding a sequence to a
multiple of C C with zero 𝒒 , 𝒌 , 𝒗 \bm{q},\bm{k},\bm{v} , β = c = 0 \beta=c=0 and log ⁡ 𝐟 = 𝟎 \log\mathbf{f}=\bm{0} neither writes nor decays. (vi) Queries are scaled by d k − 1 / 2 d_{k}^{-1/2}
inside the kernel; the scale is immaterial after the RMSNorm of the block.
In the supplied code, 𝑲 ^ , 𝑲 ¯ , 𝑲 ˘ , 𝑸 ^ \widehat{\bm{K}},\bar{\bm{K}},\breve{\bm{K}},\widehat{\bm{Q}} are k_up , k_dn , k_tail ,
q_up ; 𝑮 k ​ k , 𝑮 v ​ v , 𝑮 q ​ k \bm{G}^{kk},\bm{G}^{vv},\bm{G}^{qk} are A_kk ,
A_vv , A_qk ; 𝚲 \bm{\Lambda} , 𝒖 ∘ \bm{u}^{\circ} , 𝚲 β \bm{\Lambda}_{\beta} are
Tinv , u_const , Tw ; and 𝒑 \bm{p} , 𝒖 \bm{u} ,
𝑽 ~ \widetilde{\bm{V}} are p , u , uv .

Here 𝜸 ∈ ℝ C × d k \bm{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{C\times d_{k}} stacks the 𝜸 r \bm{\gamma}_{r} and all products with
it are element-wise. Two points differ from the delta-rule backward. The
values enter the system matrix 𝑵 \bm{N} through 𝑮 v ​ v \bm{G}^{vv} , so d ​ 𝑽 \mathrm{d}\bm{V}
receives a contribution through the inverse in
( A163 )–( A164 ), whereas in DeltaNet, Gated
DeltaNet and KDA the values appear only in the right-hand side. And since
the pseudo-values are u r ​ 𝒗 r u_{r}\bm{v}_{r} , the gradient with respect to the write
coefficients is the row-wise contraction d ​ 𝒖 = ( d ​ 𝑽 ~ ⊙ 𝑽 ) ​ 𝟏 \mathrm{d}\bm{u}=(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\bm{V}}\odot\bm{V})\bm{1} over the value dimension,
mirroring the forward contraction that produces 𝒑 \bm{p} . The gate factor
diag ⁡ ( 𝜷 ) \operatorname{diag}(\bm{\beta}) sits inside the Hadamard product and must be kept inside the
accumulation of d ​ 𝑵 \mathrm{d}\bm{N} , as observed for the channel-wise gates of
Gated DeltaNet-2 ( Hatamizadeh et al., 2026 ) .
A8.9 Verification
We verified Theorem A11 and
( A162 )–( A165 ) numerically in double precision
against the token-by-token recurrence ( A149 ): outputs and final
memory agree to machine precision ( ≤ 10 − 15 \leq 10^{-15} relative) for chunk
lengths C ∈ { 1 , 8 , 16 , L } C\in\{1,8,16,L\} , sequence lengths that are and are not
multiples of C C , d k ≠ d v d_{k}\neq d_{v} , nonzero initial memory, strong and weak
decay, and β r ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) \beta_{r}\in(0,1) as well as β r ∈ ( 0 , 2 ) \beta_{r}\in(0,2) ; the operator
identity ( A161 ) holds against the explicitly formed product of
d k ​ d v × d k ​ d v d_{k}d_{v}\times d_{k}d_{v} factors; the no-decay reduction of 𝑵 \bm{N} to the
Gram of 𝒂 r = 𝒌 r ⊗ 𝒗 r \bm{a}_{r}=\bm{k}_{r}\otimes\bm{v}_{r} holds; and the gradients with respect
to 𝑸 , 𝑲 , 𝑽 , 𝜷 , 𝒄 , log ⁡ 𝐟 \bm{Q},\bm{K},\bm{V},\bm{\beta},\bm{c},\log\mathbf{f} and 𝑺 0 \bm{S}_{0} agree with central
finite differences of the recurrence to 10 − 9 10^{-9} . The reference
implementations in the supplementary material are checked against the same
recurrence.

Instructions for reporting errors
We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
methods listed below:
Click the "Report Issue" (
) button, located in the page header.
Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.
Our team has already identified the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
all.
Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion , and welcome developer contributions .
